“No, sir; no, sir; no, sir!” he heard the big voice rumbling, and then, breaking into thunder, “I tell you *no*! Some o’ you men make me sick! You’d lose your confidence in Almighty God if a doodlebug flipped his hind leg at you! You say money’s tight all over the country. Well, what if it is? There’s no reason for it to be tight, and it’s not goin’ to keep *our* money tight! You’re always runnin’ to the woodshed to hide your nickels in a crack because some fool newspaper says the market’s a little skeery! You listen to every street-corner croaker and then come and set here and try to scare *me* out of a big thing! We’re *in* on this﻿—understand? I tell you there never *was* better times. These are good times and big times, and I won’t stand for any other kind o’ talk. This country’s on its feet as it never was before, and this city’s on its feet and goin’ to stay there!” And Gurney heard a series of whacks and thumps upon the desk. “ ‘Bad times’!” Sheridan vociferated, with accompanying thumps. “Rabbit talk! These times are glorious, I tell you! We’re in the promised land, and we’re goin’ to *stay* there! That’s all, gentlemen. The loan goes!”

===Chinese===
比布斯依从了那只还缠着绷带的手挥出的手势。黑绸吊带也还挂在谢里丹的脖子上，可是格尼的话也罢，疼得钻心的抽搐也罢，都不能叫谢里丹那只手安分待在吊带里。伤原本轻得很，只因他头一回挣脱绷带，便发了炎，愈合就遥遥无期。谢里丹有打手势的习惯；他“腾不出工夫来记着”——他自己这么说——自己须得小心；另外，他对自己的伤还怀着一种古怪的义愤；承认它存在的那份恭维，他不肯赏给它。

伊迪丝走后的那个星期六，格尼来到谢里丹大厦给伤口换药，并且要同谢里丹谈一谈——医生觉得这一谈已经免不得了。只是他到得比约定的时候早了些，只得在一间前厅里候上几分钟——谢里丹的办公室里正开着一场不知什么名目的董事会议。门虚掩着，漏出雪茄的烟和演说来，后者清一色出自谢里丹。格尼侧耳听着。

“不成，先生；不成，先生；不成，先生！”他听见那条大嗓门咕噜噜地滚，随即炸成了雷，“我告诉你们*不*成！你们有些人真叫我恶心！一只蚁狮朝你们蹬一下后腿，你们就要对全能的上帝也失掉信心！你们说全国银根都紧。好，紧又怎么样？没有道理紧，也决计紧不着*我们的*钱！哪家糊涂报纸说市面有点儿吓人，你们就一窝蜂往柴棚里跑，把五分镍币往板缝里塞！街角上随便哪个乌鸦嘴一叫你们都听，听完了跑来坐在这儿，想把我从一桩大买卖里吓出去！这桩买卖*有我们的份*——明白不明白？我告诉你们，从来还没有比这*更好*的年成。这是好年成、大年成，别的论调我一句不听。这个国家站得从来没有这样稳，这座城也站得稳稳的，还要一直站下去！”格尼听见桌面上接连响起一串拍击和擂捶。“‘年成坏’！”谢里丹嚷道，拳头一路跟着擂。“兔子话！我告诉你们，这个年成是辉煌的！我们已经到了应许之地，还要*一直待*在那儿！就说到这儿，先生们。贷款照放！”

===Original===
The directors came forth, flushed and murmurous, and Gurney hastened in. His guess was correct: Sheridan had been thumping the desk with his right hand. The physician scolded wearily, making good the fresh damage as best he might; and then he said what he had to say on the subject of Roscoe and Sibyl, his opinion meeting, as he expected, a warmly hostile reception. But the result of this conversation was that by telephonic command Roscoe awaited his father, an hour later, in the library at the New House.

“Gurney says your wife’s able to travel,” Sheridan said brusquely, as he came in.

“Yes.” Roscoe occupied a deep chair and sat in the dejected attitude which had become his habit. “Yes, she is.”

“Edith had to leave town, and so Sibyl thinks she’ll have to, too!”

===Chinese===
董事们鱼贯而出，一个个满面通红，嘁嘁喳喳；格尼赶紧进去。他猜得不错：谢里丹正是用右手擂的桌子。医生没好气地数落了一通，尽力把新添的损伤修补了一番；然后他把关于罗斯科和西比尔非说不可的话说了，不出所料，他的意见遭到一顿火辣辣的抵制。然而这场谈话的结果是：一道电话命令下去，一个钟头以后，罗斯科在新宅的书房里候着他父亲。

“格尼说，你媳妇能出门旅行了，”谢里丹一边进门一边生硬地说。

“是的。”罗斯科陷在一张深座大扶手椅里，坐着一副垂头丧气的姿势——那已经成了他的习惯。“是的，她能了。”

“伊迪丝不得不出城去，这么着西比尔也就想她非走不可了！”

===Original===
“Oh, I wouldn’t put it that way,” Roscoe protested, drearily.

“No, I hear *you* wouldn’t!” There was a bitter gibe in the father’s voice, and he added: “It’s a good thing she’s goin’ abroad﻿—if she’ll stay there. I shouldn’t think any of us want her here any more﻿—you least of all!”

“It’s no use your talking that way,” said Roscoe. “You won’t do any good.”

“Well, when are you comin’ back to your office?” Sheridan used a brisker, kinder tone. “Three weeks since you showed up there at all. When you goin’ to be ready to cut out whiskey and all the rest o’ the foolishness and start in again? You ought to be able to make up for a lot o’ lost time and a lot o’ spilt milk when that woman takes herself out o’ the way and lets you and all the rest of us alone.”

===Chinese===
“哦，话不能这样说法，”罗斯科有气无力地分辩。

“可不是，我听说*你*不肯这样说！”父亲的话音里带着辛辣的讥诮，他又找补一句：“她出国去倒是件好事——只要她肯待在那儿。我想咱们家里谁也不愿她再待在这儿——你顶不愿意！”

“你这样说也没有用，”罗斯科说。“没有一点用处。”

“那么，你什么时候回办公室来？”谢里丹换了一种爽利些、也和软些的口气。“你整整三个礼拜没在那儿露过面了。你打算什么时候预备戒了威士忌和其余那些糊涂事，重新干起来？等那个女人自己让开了道，不再搅你，也不再搅咱们大伙，你就该能把一大堆耽误了的工夫、一大堆泼翻了的牛奶统统补回来才是。”

===Original===
“It’s no use, father, I tell you. I know what Gurney was going to say to you. I’m not going back to the office. I’m *done*!”

“Wait a minute before you talk that way!” Sheridan began his sentry-go up and down the room. “I suppose you know it’s taken two pretty good men about sixteen hours a day to set things straight and get ’em runnin’ right again, down in your office?”

“They must be good men.” Roscoe nodded indifferently. “I thought I was doing about eight men’s work. I’m glad you found two that could handle it.”

“Look here! If I worked you it was for your own good. There are plenty men drive harder’n *I* do, and﻿—”

“Yes. There are some that break down all the other men that work with ’em. They either die, or go crazy, or have to quit, and are no use the rest of their lives. The last’s my case, I guess﻿—‘complicated by domestic difficulties’!”

===Chinese===
“没有用，父亲，我告诉你。格尼要跟你说什么，我知道。我不回办公室去了。我*到头了*！”

“说这种话之前，先等一等！”谢里丹开始在屋里踱起步来，哨兵似的走来走去。“想来你知道，你那间办公室，是两个挺不赖的人，每天约莫十六个钟头，才把事情扳正、重新运转起来的？”

“那他们准是好人手。”罗斯科漠然点了点头。“我原当自己干的是约莫八个人的活。你们找着两个顶得下来的，我很高兴。”

“你听着！我要是逼你逼得紧，那是为你自己好。比我*逼*得更凶的人多的是，而且——”

“是啊。有些人把同他们共事的人统统熬垮了。那些人不是死掉，就是疯掉，要么就得退出，后半辈子成了废物。末了那一种就是我的情形，我猜——‘再兼家室之累’！”

===Original===
“You set there and tell me you give up?” Sheridan’s voice shook, and so did the gesticulating hand which he extended appealingly toward the despondent figure. “Don’t do it, Roscoe! Don’t say it! Say you’ll come down there again and be a man! This woman ain’t goin’ to trouble you any more. The work ain’t goin’ to hurt you if you haven’t got her to worry you, and you can get shut o’ this nasty whiskey-guzzlin’; it ain’t fastened on you yet. Don’t say﻿—”

“It’s no use on earth,” Roscoe mumbled. “No use on earth.”

“Look here! If you want another month’s vacation﻿—”

“I know Gurney told you, so what’s the use talking about ‘vacations’?”

===Chinese===
“你坐在那儿，告诉我你要撒手了？”谢里丹的声音发颤，那只朝着沮丧的身影伸过去的、比划个不停的手也发颤。“别这样，罗斯科！别说这话！说你要回到那边去，做一条汉子！这个女人不会再给你麻烦了。只要你没有她搅得心烦，活儿伤不着你，你也戒得掉这恶心的灌威士忌的毛病；那毛病还没在你身上扎下根呢。别说——”

“压根儿没有用，”罗斯科咕哝道。“压根儿没有用。”

“你听着！你要是还想再歇一个月的假——”

“我知道是格尼告诉你的，那还谈什么‘假期’？”

===Original===
“Gurney!” Sheridan vociferated the name savagely. “It’s Gurney, Gurney, Gurney! Always Gurney! I don’t know what the world’s comin’ to with everybody runnin’ around squealin’, ‘The doctor says this,’ and, ‘The doctor says that’! It makes me sick! How’s this country expect to get its Work done if Gurney and all the other old nanny-goats keep up this blattin’﻿—‘Oh, oh! Don’t lift that stick o’ wood; you’ll ruin your *nerves*!’ So he says you got ‘nervous exhaustion induced by overwork and emotional strain.’ They always got to stick the Work in if they see a chance! I reckon you did have the ‘emotional strain,’ and that’s all’s the matter with you. You’ll be over it soon’s this woman’s gone, and Work’s the very thing to make you quit frettin’ about her.”

“Did Gurney tell you I was fit to work?”

“Shut up!” Sheridan bellowed. “I’m so sick o’ that man’s name I feel like shootin’ anybody that says it to me!” He fumed and chafed, swearing indistinctly, then came and stood before his son. “Look here; do you think you’re doin’ the square thing by me? Do you? How much you worth?”

===Chinese===
“格尼！”谢里丹恶狠狠地嚷出这个名字。“格尼，格尼，格尼！张口格尼，闭口格尼！人人都满世界乱跑，尖着嗓子喊‘医生说这个’‘医生说那个’，我不知道这世道要变成什么样！真叫我恶心！要是格尼和一班别的老母山羊照这样咩咩叫个不停——‘哦哟哟！别搬那根木头，会伤了你的*神经*！’——这个国家还指望把活儿干成吗？所以他说你得了‘操劳过度兼情绪紧张引起的神经衰竭’。只要逮着机会，他们就非得把活儿捎带上一笔！我估摸你倒是真有过那场‘情绪紧张’，你的毛病也就这么一点。这女人一走，你就好了；而且活儿正是叫你不再为她烦心的对症药。”

“格尼跟你说我还能干活了吗？”

“住口！”谢里丹吼道。“那人的名字我听得烦透了，谁再对我提一遍，我就想崩了谁！”他气得直哼哼，焦躁恼怒，含混地骂了几句，然后走过来，站在儿子面前。“你听着；你自个儿掂量掂量，你这样待我公道吗？公道吗？你值多少钱？”

===Original===
“I’ve got between seven and eight thousand a year clear, of my own, outside the salary. That much is mine whether I work or not.”

“It is? You could’a pulled it out without me, I suppose you think, at your age?”

“No. But it’s mine, and it’s enough.”

“My Lord! It’s about what a Congressman gets, and you want to quit there! I suppose you think you’ll get the rest when *I* kick the bucket, and all you have to do is lay back and wait! You let me tell you right here, you’ll never see one cent of it. You go out o’ business now, and what would you know about handlin’ it five or ten or twenty years from now? Because I intend to *stay* here a little while yet, my boy! They’d either get it away from you or you’d sell for a nickel and let it be split up and﻿—” He whirled about, marched to the other end of the room, and stood silent a moment. Then he said, solemnly: “Listen. If you go out now, you leave me in the lurch, with nothin’ on God’s green earth to depend on but your brother﻿—and you know what he is. I’ve depended on you for it *all* since Jim died. Now you’ve listened to that dam’ doctor, and he says maybe you won’t ever be as good a man as you were, and that certainly you won’t be for a year or so﻿—probably more. Now, that’s all a lie. Men don’t break down that way at your age. Look at *me*! And *I* tell you, you can shake this thing off. All you need is a little *get*-up and a little gumption. Men don’t go away for *years* and then come back into *moving* businesses like ours﻿—they lose the strings. And if you could, I won’t let you﻿—if you lay down on me now, I won’t﻿—and that’s because if you lay down you prove you ain’t the man I thought you were.” He cleared his throat and finished quietly: “Roscoe, will you take a month’s vacation and come back and go to it?”

===Chinese===
“除开薪水，我自己每年有七千到八千块的净进项。这份产业是我的，我干不干活它都是我的。”

“是吗？照你想，凭你那点年纪，没有我你也能挣出这份家当来？”

“挣不出。可它是我的，也尽够了。”

“我的老天！那也就够个国会议员的进项，你竟想撂下不干就到头了！照你想，等*我*蹬了腿儿，剩下的都是你的，你只消往椅背上一靠，等着就行！好吧，就在这儿叫我把话说清楚，那一个铜板你也别想见着。你这会儿退出买卖去，过五年、十年、二十年，你懂怎么料理它？因为我打算还在这儿*待*一阵子呢，我的孩子！他们不是把产业从你手里弄走，就是你把它贱卖成五分镍币，由着它七零八落分掉，还有——”他猛地转过身，大步走到屋子那一头，静立了片刻。然后他郑重其事地说：“听着。你这会儿一走，就是把我撂在半道上，这上帝的青葱大地上，除了你弟弟，我再没有别的可指靠——他是什么货色，你自己知道。吉姆死后，这一切我*全*指望着你。如今你听了那个该死的医生的话，他说你也许永远恢复不到从前那副人样了，而且至少一年上下——兴许还不止——肯定不行。哼，这通通是鬼话。你这点年纪的人，不会那样垮掉。瞧瞧*我*！再有，*我*告诉你，这点事你甩得掉。你只消有一点儿*闯*劲儿，有一点儿志气。没有人离开*好几年*，回头还能插手我们这种*转个不停*的买卖——线头全丢了。就算你能，我也不依——你这会儿要是撂下我不管，我就不依——因为你要是撂下了，就证明你不是我素来认作的那号人。”他清了清嗓子，末了平平静静地说：“罗斯科，你歇一个月的假，回来接着干，好不好？”

===Original===
“No,” said Roscoe, listlessly. “I’m through.”

“All right,” said Sheridan. He picked up the evening paper from a table, went to a chair by the fire and sat down, his back to his son. “Goodbye.”

Roscoe rose, his head hanging, but there was a dull relief in his eyes. “Best I can do,” he muttered, seeming about to depart, yet lingering. “I figure it out a good deal like this,” he said. “I didn’t *know* my job was any strain, and I managed all right, but from what Gur﻿—from what I hear, I was just up to the limit of my nerves from overwork, and the﻿—the trouble at home was the extra strain that’s fixed me the way I am. I tried to brace, so I could stand the work and the trouble too, on whiskey﻿—and that put the finish to me! I﻿—I’m not hitting it as hard as I was for a while, and I reckon pretty soon, if I can get to feeling a little more energy, I better try to quit entirely﻿—I don’t know. I’m all in﻿—and the doctor says so. I thought I was running along fine up to a few months ago, but all the time I was ready to bust, and didn’t know it. Now, then, I don’t want you to blame Sibyl, and if I were you I wouldn’t speak of her as ‘that woman,’ because she’s your daughter-in-law and going to stay that way. She didn’t do anything wicked. It was a shock to me, and I don’t deny it, to find what she had done﻿—encouraging that fellow to hang around her after he began trying to flirt with her, and losing her head over him the way she did. I don’t deny it was a shock and that it’ll always be a hurt inside of me I’ll never get over. But it was my fault; I didn’t understand a woman’s nature.” Poor Roscoe spoke in the most profound and desolate earnest. “A woman craves society, and gaiety, and meeting attractive people, and traveling. Well, I can’t give her the other things, but I can give her the traveling﻿—real traveling, not just going to Atlantic City or New Orleans, the way she has, two, three times. A woman has to have something in her life besides a business man. And that’s *all* I was. I never understood till I heard her talking when she was so sick, and I believe if you’d heard her then you wouldn’t speak so hard-heartedly about her; I believe you might have forgiven her like I have. That’s all. I never cared anything for any girl but her in my life, but I was so busy with business I put it ahead of her. I never *thought* about her, I was so busy thinking business. Well, this is where it’s brought us to﻿—and now when you talk about ‘business’ to me I feel the way you do when anybody talks about Gurney to you. The word ‘business’ makes me dizzy﻿—it makes me honestly sick at the stomach. I believe if I had to go downtown and step inside that office door I’d fall down on the floor, deathly sick. You talk about a ‘month’s vacation’﻿—and I get just as sick. I’m rattled﻿—I can’t plan﻿—I haven’t got any plans﻿—can’t make any, except to take my girl and get just as far away from that office as I can﻿—and stay. We’re going to Japan first, and if we﻿—”

===Chinese===
“不，”罗斯科蔫蔫地说。“我到头了。”

“好吧，”谢里丹说。他从桌上拿起晚报，走到炉边一张椅子跟前坐下，脊背朝着儿子。“再见。”

罗斯科站起身，脑袋耷拉着，眼睛里却透出一点麻木的轻松。“我顶多做到这样了，”他咕哝着，看样子要走，却又磨蹭着不动。“我大致是这么个盘算，”他说。“我*不知道*我的活儿有什么熬人，我对付得挺好；可是照格——照我听来的话，我因为操劳过度，神经已经绷到了头，家里那桩——那桩麻烦，就是把我弄成现在这模样的额外重压。我想撑住，好把活儿和麻烦一并扛下来，就靠威士忌——这一下就把我断送了！我——我这阵子灌得不像先前那阵子那么凶了，我寻思，快则不久，等我缓过来一点儿精神，顶好索性全戒了——我说不好。我整个人垮了——医生也这么说。几个月以前，我一直当自己顺顺当当地跑着，其实我随时都要炸了，只是自己不知道。那么，如今我不许你怪西比尔，我要是你，也不会管她叫‘那个女人’，因为她是你的儿媳妇，往后也还是。她没做什么伤天害理的事。她干下的那些事——那家伙开始跟她调笑之后，她还纵着他缠着自己，她又对他失魂落魄到那个地步——叫我受了震惊，这个我不否认。我不否认那是桩震惊，也不否认它在我心里永远是一处伤，一辈子好不了。可那是我的错；我不懂女人的天性。”可怜的罗斯科说这话时，态度最深切、最凄惶，字字都是当真的。“女人家渴慕交际，渴慕热闹，渴慕会见过瘾的人物，渴慕旅行。好吧，别的我给不了她，旅行我给得起——真正的旅行，不是像她那样上大西洋城或新奥尔良去两三回就算数的。女人的日子里，总得有一个生意人以外的什么东西。而我*仅仅*就是这么个东西。直到她病得那么厉害、听她说那些话，我才明白过来；我相信你那时要是听见了，也不会这样硬着心肠说她；我相信你兴许会像我一样饶恕她。就这些。我一辈子除了她，没对任何姑娘上过心；可我买卖忙，把买卖摆在了她前头。我心里压根儿没*想*过她，我满脑子想的都是买卖。好，这就是把我们带到的地方——如今你对我提起‘买卖’两个字，我的感觉，就跟人家对你提起格尼时你的感觉一样。‘买卖’这个字叫我发晕——叫我实实在在的恶心。我相信，要是逼我下城去，跨进那间办公室的门，我会一头栽在地板上，恶心得要死。你说什么‘歇一个月的假’——我一样地恶心。我心乱如麻——没法盘算——我什么盘算都没有——也做不出盘算，只剩一条：带上我那位姑娘，离那间办公室越远越好——然后待下来。我们先去日本，要是我们——”

===Original===
His father rustled the paper. “I said goodbye, Roscoe.”

“Goodbye,” said Roscoe, listlessly.

===Chinese===
他父亲把报纸抖得沙沙响。“我说过再见了，罗斯科。”

“再见，”罗斯科蔫蔫地说。

## XXIV / 二十四

===Original===

Sheridan waited until he heard the sound of the outer door closing; then he rose and pushed a tiny disk set in the wall. Jackson appeared.

“Has Bibbs got home from work?”

“Mist’ Bibbs? No, suh.”

“Tell him I want to see him, soon as he comes.”

“Yessuh.”

===Chinese===

谢里丹一直等到听见外面那道门关上的声音，才站起身，按了按嵌在墙上的一个小圆钮。杰克逊进来了。

“比布斯下工回来了没有？”

“比布斯先生？没呢，先生。”

“告诉他，他一回来我就要见他。”

“是咯，先生。”

===Original===

Sheridan returned to his chair and fixed his attention fiercely upon the newspaper. He found it difficult to pursue the items beyond their explanatory rubrics﻿—there was nothing unusual or startling to concentrate his attention:

“Motorman Puts Blame on Brakes. Three Killed when Car Slides.” “Burglars Make Big Haul.” “Board Works Approve Big Car-line Extension.” “Holdup Men Injure Two. Man Found in Alley, Skull Fractured.” “Sickening Story Told in Divorce Court.” “Plan New Eighteen-story Structure.” “Schoolgirl Meets Death under Automobile.” “Negro Cuts Three. One Dead.” “Life Crushed Out. Third Elevator Accident in Same Building Causes Action by Coroner.” “Declare Militia will be Menace. Polish Societies Protest to Governor in Church Rioting Case.” “Short $3,500 in Accounts, Trusted Man Kills Self with Drug.” “Found Frozen. Family Without Food or Fuel. Baby Dead when Parents Return Home from Seeking Work.” “Minister Returned from Trip Abroad Lectures on Big Future of Our City. Sees Big Improvement during Short Absence. Says No European City Holds Candle.” (Sheridan nodded approvingly here.)

===Chinese===

谢里丹回到椅子上，狠狠地把注意力钉在报纸上。他发觉那些新闻顶多能追到各自说明梗概的标题，再往下就难乎为继了——没有任何异乎寻常、耸人听闻的东西，能把他的注意力拢住：

“电车司机把罪责推给刹车。车辆滑行，三人丧命。”“窃贼做下大案。”“工务局批准电车线路大扩展。”“拦路抢劫伤二人。小巷发现男子，头骨破裂。”“离婚法庭讲出令人作呕的案情。”“拟建十八层新厦。”“女学生殒命于汽车轮下。”“黑人刀伤三人，一人身亡。”“活活压毙。同一座大厦第三起电梯事故，验尸官出面立案。”“宣称民团将成为祸患。教堂骚乱案中，波兰人社团向州长抗议。”“账目亏空 3,500 美元，深受信任的职员服毒自尽。”“冻毙。一家断了食物燃料。父母外出找活儿归来，婴孩已经死了。”“牧师海外游历归来，讲演本城前程远大。短期小别即见巨大进步。称欧洲无一城市可望其项背。”（谢里丹读到这里，赞许地点了点头。）

===Original===

Bibbs came through the hall whistling, and entered the room briskly. “Well, father, did you want me?”

“Yes. Sit down.” Sheridan got up, and Bibbs took a seat by the fire, holding out his hands to the crackling blaze, for it was cold outdoors.

“I came within seven of the shop record today,” he said. “I handled more strips than any other workman has any day this month. The nearest to me is sixteen behind.”

“There!” exclaimed his father, greatly pleased. “What’d I tell you? I’d like to hear Gurney hint again that I wasn’t right in sending you there﻿—I would just like to hear him! And you﻿—ain’t you ashamed of makin’ such a fuss about it? Ain’t you?”

“I didn’t go at it in the right spirit the other time,” Bibbs said, smiling brightly, his face ruddy in the cheerful firelight. “I didn’t know the difference it meant to like a thing.”

===Chinese===

比布斯吹着口哨穿过门厅，脚步轻快地进了屋。“哟，父亲，您找我？”

“是啊。坐下。”谢里丹站起身，比布斯在炉边坐了，朝噼啪作响的火苗伸出双手烤着，外头天冷。

“我今天离车间的纪录只差七根锌条，”他说，“我这个月经手的锌条，比哪个工人在哪一天经手的都多。离我最近的也落后十六根。”

“瞧见没有！”他父亲大为高兴，嚷道，“我怎么说来着？格尼医生再敢暗示一句，说我送你去那儿送得不对——我倒真想听听他敢！还有你——你当初为这个大闹一场，害不害臊？害不害臊？”

“上回是我自个儿的心气儿没摆对，”比布斯说，笑得一脸明朗，欢快的炉火映得他满面红光。“我先前不懂得，喜欢上一样东西，那滋味有多不一样。”

===Original===

“Well, I guess I’ve pretty thoroughly vindicated my judgement. I guess I *have*! I said the shop’d be good for you, and it was. I said it wouldn’t hurt you, and it hasn’t. It’s been just exactly what I said it would be. Ain’t that so?”

“Looks like it!” Bibbs agreed, gaily.

“Well, I’d like to know any place I been wrong, first and last! Instead o’ hurting you, it’s been the makin’ of you﻿—physically. You’re a good inch taller’n what I am, and you’d be a bigger man than what I am if you’d get some flesh on your bones; and you *are* gettin’ a little. Physically, it’s started you out to be the huskiest one o’ the whole family. Now, then, mentally﻿—that’s different. I don’t say it unkindly, Bibbs, but you got to do something for yourself mentally, just like what’s begun physically. And I’m goin’ to help you.”

Sheridan decided to sit down again. He brought his chair close to his son’s, and, leaning over, tapped Bibbs’s knee confidentially. “I got plans for you, Bibbs,” he said.

===Chinese===

“哼，我看我这回算是把我的判断证实得再彻底不过了。我看我*就是*证实了！我说过车间对你有好处，果然有好处。我说过它伤不着你，果然没伤着。事情跟我说的分毫不差。难道不是这样？”

“瞧着是！”比布斯兴高采烈地附和。

“哼，我倒想知道，打头到尾我哪一处错过！它没有伤着你，反倒成全了你——论身子骨。你比我足足高出一英寸，只要身上再长点儿肉，你就会是个比我块头更大的汉子；而且你*也*确实在长那么一点儿了。身子骨上，它已经把你往全家头一号壮汉的路上送出去了。好，再说脑力——那是另一码事。我说这个不是存心刻薄，比布斯，可你在脑力上也得替自个儿下点功夫，就跟身子骨上已经起了头的那样。我来帮你。”

谢里丹决定重新坐下。他把椅子挪到儿子跟前，俯过身去，推心置腹地拍拍比布斯的膝盖。“我给你有一套盘算，比布斯，”他说。

===Original===

Bibbs instantly looked thoroughly alarmed. He drew back. “I﻿—I’m all right now, father.”

“Listen.” Sheridan settled himself in his chair, and spoke in the tone of a reasonable man reasoning. “Listen here, Bibbs. I had another blow today, and it was a hard one and right in the face, though I *have* been expectin’ it some little time back. Well, it’s got to be met. Now I’ll be frank with you. As I said a minute ago, mentally I couldn’t ever called you exactly strong. You been a little weak both ways, most of your life. Not but what I think you *got* a mentality, if you’d learn to use it. You got willpower, I’ll say that for you. I never knew boy or man that could be stubborner﻿—never one in my life! Now, then, you’ve showed you could learn to run that machine best of any man in the shop, in no time at all. That looks to me like you could learn to do other things. I don’t deny but what it’s an encouragin’ sign. I don’t deny that, at all. Well, that helps me to think the case ain’t so hopeless as it looks. You’re all I got to meet this blow with, but maybe you ain’t as poor material as I thought. Your tellin’ me about comin’ within seven strips of the shop’s record today looks to me like encouragin’ information brought in at just about the right time. Now, then, I’m goin’ to give you a raise. I wanted to send you straight on up through the shops﻿—a year or two, maybe﻿—but I can’t do it. I lost Jim, and now I’ve lost Roscoe. He’s quit. He’s laid down on me. If he ever comes back at all, he’ll be a long time pickin’ up the strings, and, anyway, he ain’t the man I thought he was. I can’t count on him. I got to have *somebody I know* I can count on. And I’m down to this: you’re my last chance. Bibbs, I got to learn you to use what brains you got and see if we can’t develop ’em a little. Who knows? And I’m goin’ to put my time in on it. I’m goin’ to take you right downtown with *me*, and I won’t be hard on you if you’re a little slow at first. And I’m goin’ to do the big thing for you. I’m goin’ to make you feel you got to do the big thing for me, in return. I’ve vindicated my policy with you about the shop, and now I’m goin’ to turn right around and swing you ’way over ahead of where the other boys started, and I’m goin’ to make an appeal to your ambition that’ll make you dizzy!” He tapped his son on the knee again. “Bibbs, I’m goin’ to start you off this way: I’m goin’ to make you a director in the Pump Works Company; I’m goin’ to make you vice-president of the Realty Company and a vice-president of the Trust Company!”

===Chinese===

比布斯登时满脸惊惶，直往后缩。“我——我现在好好的，父亲。”

“听着。”谢里丹在椅子里坐定，拿出一个通情达理的人讲道理的腔调。“你听着，比布斯。我今天又挨了一记打击，又狠，又正打在脸上，虽说这些日子我也*确实*料着点儿了。好吧，这一击总得接下。现在我跟你交个底。就像我一分钟前说的，论脑力，我从来不能说你算得上强。你这半辈子，两头都有点儿弱。倒不是我觉得你*没有*脑子，只要肯学着用就有。意志力你是有的，这一条我替你说句公道话。比我生平认识的哪个男孩、哪个男人都犟——一辈子没见过第二个！好，如今你已经显出来了，你没多大会儿工夫就学得比车间里哪个工人都强，把那台机器开好了。照我看，这说明旁的你也能学着做。我不否认这是个鼓舞人的苗头。我一点也不否认。嗯，这让我觉得，事情还不像看上去那样没指望。接这一击，我就剩你这一份家底了，可你这材料，也许没我想的那么不济。你今天告诉我，你离车间的纪录只差七根锌条——在我听来，这份鼓舞人的消息来得正是时候。好，那么，我打算提拔你一下。我本想让你顺着车间一级一级直接升上去——也许一年两年——可如今办不到了。我丢了吉姆，如今又丢了罗斯科。他不干了。他撂了我的挑子。就算他真回头，也得拖上老半天才能把那些线头拾起来；况且他到底不是我先前当的那号人。他指靠不上了。我得有个*我明知*靠得住的人。如今落到这一步：你是我最后一注。比布斯，我得教会你使出你那点脑子来，看看能不能把它再开发开发。谁知道呢？我打算把我自个儿的时光搭进去。我要带你*跟我*直接下市区，起头你要是慢了点儿，我也不难为你。我要替你办一桩大事。我要让你觉得，你也得替我办一桩大事，算是回报。在车间这件事上，我的主张已经在你身上得了证明；如今我要掉转头来，把你一下子抡到比你两个哥哥起步还要靠前老远的地方，再朝你的雄心猛激一下，激得你晕头转向！”他又在儿子膝上拍了一拍。“比布斯，我打算这样给你起头：我要让你当泵厂公司（Pump Works Company）的董事，当地产公司的副总裁，再当信托公司的副总裁！”

===Original===

Bibbs jumped to his feet, blanched. “Oh no!” he cried.

Sheridan took his dismay to be the excitement of sudden joy. “Yes, sir! And there’s some pretty fat little salaries goes with those vice-presidencies, and a pinch o’ stock in the Pump Company with the directorship. You thought I was pretty mean about the shop﻿—oh, I know you did!﻿—but you see the old man can play it both ways. And so right now, the minute you’ve begun to make good the way I wanted you to, I deal from the new deck. And I’ll keep on handin’ it out bigger and bigger every time you show me you’re big enough to play the hand I deal you. I’m startin’ you with a pretty big one, my boy!”

“But I don’t﻿—I don’t﻿—I don’t want it!” Bibbs stammered.

===Chinese===

比布斯一跃而起，脸色刷白。“哦，不行！”他喊道。

谢里丹把他这份惊惶只当是喜从天降的激动。“一点不错！那两个副总裁还各带一份相当肥的小薪水，董事的位子上还搭着泵厂公司一小撮零星股票。你先前准觉得我在车间那件事上挺刻薄——哦，我知道你准这么想！——可你瞧，老头子两头都玩得转。这不，眼下你刚刚照我的心愿开始做出点样子，我就换了一副新牌来发。往后只要你显给我看，你够大，打得起我发你的牌，我就一回发得比一回更大。孩子，我给你起手的这一副就不算小喽！”

“可是我不——我不——我不想要！”比布斯结结巴巴地说。

===Original===

“What’d you say?” Sheridan thought he had not heard aright.

“I don’t want it, father. I thank you﻿—I do thank you﻿—”

Sheridan looked perplexed. “What’s the matter with you? Didn’t you understand what I was tellin’ you?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===

“你说什么？”谢里丹只当自己没听真切。

“我不想要，父亲。我谢您——我真的谢您——”

谢里丹一脸困惑。“你是怎么了？我方才跟你说的那些，你没听懂？”

“听懂了。”

===Original===

“You sure? I reckon you didn’t. I offered﻿—”

“I know, I know! But I can’t take it.”

“What’s the matter with you?” Sheridan was half amazed, half suspicious. “Your head feel funny?”

“I’ve never been quite so sane in my life,” said Bibbs, “as I have lately. And I’ve got just what I want. I’m living exactly the right life. I’m earning my daily bread, and I’m happy in doing it. My wages are enough. I don’t want any more money, and I don’t deserve any﻿—”

===Chinese===

“你拿得准？我看你就是没听懂。我给你的是——”

“我知道，我知道！可是我不能要。”

“你到底是怎么了？”谢里丹半是惊愕，半是起疑。“你脑袋发晕是吧？”

“我这辈子从来没有像近来这样神志清明，”比布斯说，“我得到的正是我想要的。我过着恰恰对路的日子。我在挣我的日常面包，而且挣得快活。我的工钱尽够了。更多的钱我不想要，也不配要——”

===Original===

“Damnation!” Sheridan sprang up. “You’ve turned Socialist! You been listening to those fellows down there, and you﻿—”

“No, sir. I think there’s a great deal in what they say, but that isn’t it.”

Sheridan tried to restrain his growing fury, and succeeded partially. “Then what is it? What’s the matter?”

“Nothing,” his son returned, nervously. “Nothing﻿—except that I’m content. I don’t want to change anything.”

===Chinese===

“该死！”谢里丹蹦了起来。“你变成社会主义者啦！你听车间底下那帮家伙的话，听得入了套，你——”

“没有，先生。他们说的话里头有很大道理，这我也认，可不是为了这个。”

谢里丹竭力按捺越烧越旺的怒火，总算按住了一半。“那是为了什么？究竟是怎么回事？”

“没什么，”儿子怯生生地答道。“没什么——只不过我心满意足了。我什么也不想改动。”

===Original===

“Why not?”

Bibbs had the incredible folly to try to explain. “I’ll tell you, father, if I can. I know it may be hard to understand﻿—”

“Yes, I think it may be,” said Sheridan, grimly. “What you say usually is a *little* that way. Go on!”

Perturbed and distressed, Bibbs rose instinctively; he felt himself at every possible disadvantage. He was a sleeper clinging to a dream﻿—a rough hand stretched to shake him and waken him. He went to a table and made vague drawings upon it with a finger, and as he spoke he kept his eyes lowered. “You weren’t altogether right about the shop﻿—that is, in one way you weren’t, father.” He glanced up apprehensively. Sheridan stood facing him, expressionless, and made no attempt to interrupt. “That’s difficult to explain,” Bibbs continued, lowering his eyes again, to follow the tracings of his finger. “I﻿—I believe the shop might have done for me this time if I hadn’t﻿—if something hadn’t helped me to﻿—oh, not only to bear it, but to be happy in it. Well, I *am* happy in it. I want to go on just as I am. And of all things on earth that I don’t want, I don’t want to live a business life﻿—I don’t want to be drawn into it. I don’t think it *is* living﻿—and now I *am* living. I have the healthful toil﻿—and I can think. In business as important as yours I couldn’t think anything but business. I don’t﻿—I don’t think making money is worth while.”

===Chinese===

“为什么不想？”

比布斯竟愚不可及，想要解释一番。“我跟您说，父亲，要是我能说清楚的话。我知道这也许不容易懂——”

“是啊，我也觉得不容易，”谢里丹冷冷地说。“你说的话向来总*有*点儿那个。说下去！”

比布斯心慌意乱、苦恼不堪，本能地站起身来；他觉得自己处处都陷在下风。他像一个攀住梦不肯撒手的酣睡人——一只粗硬的手已经伸过来，要摇他醒来。他走到一张桌子跟前，用手指在桌面上心不在焉地画着道道，说话时眼睛一直垂着。“关于车间，您也没能全说对——就是说，有一层上头您没说对，父亲。”他提心吊胆地抬眼瞟了一下。谢里丹正面冲他站着，脸上没有一点表情，也不打算打断他。“这可不好解释，”比布斯接着说，眼睛又垂下去，追着自己指头画下的道道。“我——我相信，要不是——要不是有什么东西帮了我——哦，不只是帮我忍受它，还帮我在里头过得快活——车间这一回没准儿就把我毁了。可我*是*快活的。我想就照现在这样过下去。天底下凡是我不想要的东西里头，顶顶不想要的就是过买卖人的生活——我不想被拽进去。我不觉得那*算*活着——而如今我这才*是*活着。我有益于健康的劳作——而且我能思考。在您那样要紧的买卖里，除了买卖我什么也思考不了。我不——我不觉得挣钱这事情值得。”

===Original===

“Go on,” said Sheridan, curtly, as Bibbs paused timidly.

“It hasn’t seemed to get anywhere, that I can see,” said Bibbs. “You think this city is rich and powerful﻿—but what’s the use of its being rich and powerful? They don’t teach the children any more in the schools because the city is rich and powerful. They teach them more than they used to because some people﻿—not rich and powerful people﻿—have thought the thoughts to teach the children. And yet when you’ve been reading the paper I’ve heard you objecting to the children being taught anything except what would help them to make money. You said it was wasting the taxes. You want them taught to make a living, but not to live. When I was a little boy this wasn’t an ugly town; now it’s hideous. What’s the use of being big just to be hideous? I mean I don’t think all this has meant really going ahead﻿—it’s just been getting bigger and dirtier and noisier. Wasn’t the whole country happier and in many ways wiser when it was smaller and cleaner and quieter and kinder? I know you think I’m an utter fool, father, but, after all, though, aren’t business and politics just the housekeeping part of life? And wouldn’t you despise a woman that not only made her housekeeping her ambition, but did it so noisily and dirtily that the whole neighborhood was in a continual turmoil over it? And suppose she talked and thought about her housekeeping all the time, and was always having additions built to her house when she couldn’t keep clean what she already had; and suppose, with it all, she made the house altogether unpeaceful and unlivable﻿—”

===Chinese===

“说下去，”谢里丹生硬地说；比布斯怯怯地顿住了。

“照我看，它并没走到哪儿去，”比布斯说。“您觉得这座城有钱有势——可有钱有势又顶什么用呢？学校里并没有因为城里有钱有势，就多教孩子们一点什么。他们教的比从前多了，是因为有些人——不是有钱有势的人——想出了那些可以教给孩子们的道理。可我听您读报纸的时候说过，孩子们学这学那，凡是不帮着挣钱的，您一概反对。您说那是白糟蹋税款。您要他们学会谋生，可不要他们学会生活。我小时候，这座城还不算丑；如今它是狰狞了。只为狰狞而求大，又有什么用处？我是说，我觉得这一切并不当真意味着往前走——它不过是越变越大、越变越脏、越变越吵罢了。当初全国又小、又干净、又安静、又厚道的时候，不是更快活，在许多方面也更明白么？我知道您拿我当个十足的傻蛋，父亲，可说到底，生意和政治，不外乎生活的管家那一部分吧？一个女人不光把管家当成自己的雄心，还管得又吵又脏，搅得四邻八舍为她终日骚动不休——您难道会瞧得起她么？再假设她一天到晚想的说的全是管家，自己已有的屋子都收拾不干净，还总在添造新屋；再假设到头来，她把整个家弄得不得安宁、没法住人——”

===Original===

“Just one minute!” Sheridan interrupted, adding, with terrible courtesy, “If you will permit me? Have you ever been right about anything?”

“I don’t quite﻿—”

“I ask the simple question: Have you ever been right about anything whatever in the course of your life? Have you ever been right upon any subject or question you’ve thought about and talked about? Can you mention one single time when you were proved to be right?”

He was flourishing the bandaged hand as he spoke, but Bibbs said only, “If I’ve always been wrong before, surely there’s more chance that I’m right about this. It seems reasonable to suppose something would be due to bring up my average.”

===Chinese===

“你给我稍等一分钟！”谢里丹打断他，又添了一句，客气得怕人：“容我问一句？你这辈子有哪一回对过？”

“我不大——”

“我问一个简简单单的问题：你这辈子从头到尾，随便哪件事，有哪一回对过？你想过、谈过的任何一个题目、任何一个问题，有哪一件上头你对过？你可指得出哪怕一次来，证明你是对的？”

他说这话时挥舞着那只缠了胶布的手，比布斯只答了一句：“要是我从前一直错，那这一回我对的指望反倒更大些。总该有一回对的来给我拉拉平均数，这么想不算不讲道理吧。”

===Original===

“Yes, I thought you wouldn’t see the point. And there’s another you probably couldn’t see, but I’ll take the liberty to mention it. You been balkin’ all your life. Pretty much everything I ever wanted you to do, you’d let out *some* kind of a holler, like you are now﻿—and yet I can’t seem to remember once when you didn’t have to lay down and do what I said. But go on with your remarks about our city and the business of this country. Go on!”

“I don’t want to be a part of it,” said Bibbs, with unwonted decision. “I want to keep to myself, and I’m doing it now. I couldn’t, if I went down there with you. I’d be swallowed into it. I don’t care for money enough to﻿—”

===Chinese===

“对了，我就知道你抓不着要点。还有一层你八成也抓不着，可我斗胆提一提。你这辈子一直在犯倔。我想叫你做的事，差不多每一样你都要*闹出*点动静来，就像眼下这样——可我竟记不起有哪一回，你末了不是躺倒服输、照我说的去办。不过你还是接着谈咱们的城、谈这个国家的买卖吧。接着谈！”

“我不想做它的一分子，”比布斯说，口气破天荒地斩钉截铁。“我想守着我自个儿，眼下我正守着呢。要是跟您下那儿去，我就守不成了。我会被它一口吞进去。我对钱的瘾没有大到——”

===Original===

“No,” his father interrupted, still dangerously quiet. “You’ve never had to earn a living. Anybody could tell that by what you say. Now, let me remind you: you’re sleepin’ in a pretty good bed; you’re eatin’ pretty fair food; you’re wearin’ pretty fine clothes. Just suppose one o’ these noisy housekeepers﻿—me, for instance﻿—decided to let you do your own housekeepin’. May I ask what your proposition would be?”

“I’m earning nine dollars a week,” said Bibbs, sturdily. “It’s enough. I shouldn’t mind at all.”

“Who’s payin’ you that nine dollars a week?”

“My work!” Bibbs answered. “And I’ve done so well on that clipping-machine I believe I could work up to fifteen or even twenty a week at another job. I could be a fair plumber in a few months, I’m sure. I’d rather have a trade than be in business﻿—I should, infinitely!”

===Chinese===

“不对，”父亲打断他，声气依旧平静，平静得吓人。“你这辈子从来用不着自己挣饭吃。谁听你这些话都听得出来。好，我提醒你一句：你睡的是相当好的床，吃的是相当不坏的饭，穿的是相当讲究的衣裳。不妨设想，这些吵吵嚷嚷的管家婆里头有一个——比方说我——打定主意叫你自个儿管自个儿的家去。可否请问，你那套章程又是什么？”

“我一礼拜挣九块钱，”比布斯硬邦邦地说。“尽够了。我一点儿也不在乎。”

“那一礼拜九块钱是谁付给你的？”

“我自个儿的活儿！”比布斯答道。“再说那台裁切机我开得那么好，我信得过换个活儿，我能挣到一礼拜十五块，甚至二十块。要不上几个月，我准能当个不赖的管子匠。我宁可有门手艺，也不做买卖——一千个宁愿！”

===Original===

“You better set about learnin’ one pretty dam’ quick!” But Sheridan struggled with his temper and again was partially successful in controlling it. “You better learn a trade over Sunday, because you’re either goin’ down with me to my office Monday morning﻿—or﻿—you can go to plumbing!”

“All right,” said Bibbs, gently. “I can get along.”

Sheridan raised his hands sardonically, as in prayer. “O God,” he said, “this boy was crazy enough before he began to earn his nine dollars a week, and now his money’s gone to his head! Can’t You do nothin’ for him?” Then he flung his hands apart, palms outward, in a furious gesture of dismissal. “Get out o’ this room! You got a skull that’s thicker’n a whale’s thighbone, but it’s cracked spang all the way across! You hated the machine-shop so bad when I sent you there, you went and stayed sick for over two years﻿—and now, when I offer to take you out of it and give you the mint, you holler for the shop like a calf for its mammy! You’re cracked! Oh, but I got a fine layout here! One son died, one quit, and one’s a loon! The loon’s all I got left! H. P. Ellersly’s wife had a crazy brother, and they undertook to keep him at the house. First morning he was there he walked straight though a ten-dollar plate-glass window out into the yard. He says, ‘Oh, look at the pretty dandelion!’ That’s what you’re doin’! You want to spend your life sayin’, ‘Oh, look at the pretty dandelion!’ and you don’t care a tinker’s dam’ what you bust! Well, mister, loon or no loon, cracked and crazy or whatever you are, I’ll take you with me Monday morning, and I’ll work you and learn you﻿—yes, and I’ll lam you, if I got to﻿—until I’ve made something out of you that’s fit to be called a business man! I’ll keep at you while I’m able to stand, and if I have to lay down to die I’ll be whisperin’ at you till they get the embalmin’-fluid into me! Now go on, and don’t let me hear from you again till you can come and tell me you’ve waked up, you poor, pitiful, dandelion-pickin’ *sleep*walker!”

===Chinese===

“那你顶好赶紧学一门，越快越好！”谢里丹压着火气，这一回又算按住了一半。“你顶好趁一个礼拜天就把手艺学成，因为礼拜一一早，你要么跟我下办公室去——要么——你就干你的管子匠去！”

“好吧，”比布斯温温和和地说。“我过得下去。”

谢里丹讥诮地扬起双手，像做祷告似的。“哦，上帝啊，”他说，“这孩子没挣那一礼拜九块钱以前就够疯的了，如今这点钱又冲上了他的脑门！你就不能替他做点什么吗？”说罢他把双手猛地一分，掌心朝外，做出一个怒不可遏的轰赶手势。“滚出这间屋子！你长着一颗比鲸鱼大腿骨还厚的脑壳，可它偏偏从头到尾正正地裂穿了一道！当初我送你进机器车间，你恨得那样儿，一口气病了两年多——如今我提出把你从那儿捞出来，把造币厂捧给你，你倒哭喊着要车间，像牛犊子找娘！你脑壳裂了！哦，瞧瞧我这副好局面！一个儿子死了，一个不干了，剩下一个疯子！疯子就是我剩下的全部家当！H·P·埃勒斯利（H. P. Ellersly）的太太娘家有个疯兄弟，一家人决意把他养在宅子里。他到的头一天早上，就径直穿过一块十块钱的厚玻璃窗，走进了院子。他说：‘哦，瞧那棵多好看的蒲公英！’你眼下干的就是这个！你想一辈子念叨着‘哦，瞧那棵多好看的蒲公英！’过日子，自己砸烂什么都不心疼！好啦，先生，疯子也罢，脑壳裂了的疯子也罢，随你是什么，礼拜一一早我照旧带你走。我来使唤你、调教你——对，该揍我也会揍你——非把你折腾出个名堂来，配得上叫一声买卖人不可！只要我站得起来，我就一天揪住你不放；哪天我躺倒等死，我也要冲你嘀咕个没完，直到他们把防腐药水灌进我身子！滚吧，再别叫我听见你的信儿，除非你能来告诉我你醒了——你这可怜、可悲、摘蒲公英的*梦游*鬼！”

===Original===

Bibbs gave him a queer look. There was something like reproach in it, for once; but there was more than that﻿—he seemed to be startled by his father’s last word.

===Chinese===

比布斯古怪地望了他一眼。这一回，那目光里竟有几分像是责备的意思；可还不止于此——他像是叫他父亲末了那个词吓了一跳。

## XXV / 二十五

===Original===
There was sleet that evening, with a whopping wind, but neither this storm nor that other which so imminently threatened him held place in the consciousness of Bibbs Sheridan when he came once more to the presence of Mary. All was right in his world as he sat with her, reading Maurice Maeterlinck’s *Alladine and Palomides*. The sorrowful light of the gas-jet might have been May morning sunshine flashing amber and rose through the glowing windows of the Sainte-Chapelle, it was so bright for Bibbs. And while the zinc-eater held out to bring him such golden nights as these, all the king’s horses and all the king’s men might not serve to break the spell.

Bibbs read slowly, but in a reasonable manner, as if he were talking; and Mary, looking at him steadily from beneath her curved fingers, appeared to discover no fault. It had grown to be her habit to look at him whenever there was an opportunity. It may be said, in truth, that while they were together, and it was light, she looked at him all the time.

When he came to the end of *Alladine and Palomides* they were silent a little while, considering together; then he turned back the pages and said: “There’s something I want to read over. This:”

===Chinese===
那天晚上落着冻雨，风刮得泼天也似；可比布斯·谢里丹再度来到玛丽跟前的时候，无论是这场风暴，还是那另一场眼看就要劈头压到他身上的祸事，都没能在他的意识里占得一席之地。他坐在她身边，读着莫里斯·梅特林克（Maurice Maeterlinck）的《阿拉丁与帕洛米德斯》（Alladine and Palomides），他的世界里样样都好。煤气灯那点哀伤的光，简直就像五月清晨的阳光，透过圣礼拜堂（Sainte-Chapelle）绚烂的彩窗闪动着琥珀色与玫瑰色——在比布斯眼里，它就是这么亮。趁着那老吞锌的家伙还容他活着，还肯送给他这样金子也似的夜晚，纵把国王所有的马和国王所有的人一齐调来，也破不了这道魔咒。

比布斯读得慢，却读得从容自然，像是在说话一般；玛丽拢着手指，从指缝底下定定地望他，似乎挑不出一点错来。只要一有机会便望着他，这已经成了她的习惯。说实在的，不妨这么说：但凡两个人在一处，天又亮着，她就没有一刻不在望他。

读到《阿拉丁与帕洛米德斯》的末尾，两个人静了一会儿，一同想心思；随后他翻回前面的书页，说道：“有一段我想再念一遍。是这一段：”

===Original===
> You would think I threw a window open on the dawn.﻿ ﻿… She has a soul that can be seen around her﻿—that takes you in its arms like an ailing child and without saying anything to you consoles you for everything.﻿ ﻿… I shall never understand it all. I do not know how it can all be, but my knees bend in spite of me when I speak of it.﻿ ﻿…

He stopped and looked at her.

“You boy!” said Mary, not very clearly.

===Chinese===
> 你会以为我朝黎明推开了一扇窗。……她有一个看得见的灵魂，在她周身环绕——那灵魂把你搂进怀里，像搂一个病中的孩子，什么也不对你说，却把你的一切都替你慰平了。……这一切我永远无法全然明白。我不知道这一切怎么会是这样，可我一说起它，我的双膝便不由自主地弯了下来。……

他停下，望着她。

“你这孩子！”玛丽说，吐字不大分明。

===Original===
“Oh yes,” he returned. “But it’s true﻿—especially my knees!”

“You boy!” she murmured again, blushing charmingly. “You might read another line over. The first time I ever saw you, Bibbs, you were looking into a mirror. Do it again. But you needn’t read it﻿—I can give it to you: ‘A little Greek slave that came from the heart of Arcady!’ ”

“*I!* I’m one of the hands at the Pump Works﻿—and going to stay one, unless I have to decide to study plumbing.”

===Chinese===
“哦，是的，”他应道。“可这是真话——尤其是我的膝盖！”

“你这孩子！”她又低低说了一遍，羞得动人。“你再念一行吧。我头一回见到你，比布斯，你正照着镜子。再来一遍好了。不过你不必念——我能替你背出来：‘一个从阿卡狄亚腹地走出来的小小的希腊奴隶！’”

“*我*！我是泵厂的一个伙计——还得一直当下去，除非我非得下决心去学水暖不可。”

===Original===
“No.” She shook her head. “You love and want what’s beautiful and delicate and serene; it’s really art that you want in your life, and have always wanted. You seemed to me, from the first, the most wistful person I had ever known, and that’s what you were wistful for.”

Bibbs looked doubtful and more wistful than ever; but after a moment or two the matter seemed to clarify itself to him. “Why, no,” he said; “I wanted something else more than that. I wanted you.”

“And here I am!” she laughed, completely understanding. “I think we’re like those two in The Cloister and the Hearth. I’m just the rough Burgundian crossbow man, Denys, who followed that gentle Gerard and told everybody that the devil was dead.”

===Chinese===
“不。”她摇头。“你爱的是美的、精微的、安宁的东西，想要的也是这些；你的生活里真正想要的其实是艺术，从来想要的都是艺术。在我眼里，你打一开始就是我平生见过的最惹人怜惜的人，而你渴念的，正是这个。”

比布斯一脸疑疑惑惑，显得比什么时候都更惹人怜惜；可过了一两分钟，这件事在他心里似乎自己亮堂起来了。“嗳，不是的，”他说；“比那个更要紧的，我想要的是另一样东西。我想要的是你。”

“我这不来了嘛！”她笑道，全然明白他的意思。“我看咱们就像《修道院与炉边》（The Cloister and the Hearth）里的那一对。我就是那个粗手粗脚的勃艮第弩手丹尼斯（Denys），一路跟着那位温文的杰勒德（Gerard），逢人便说魔鬼已经死了。”

===Original===
“He isn’t, though,” said Bibbs, as a hoarse little bell in the next room began a series of snappings which proved to be ten, upon count. “He gets into the clock whenever I’m with you.” And, sighing deeply he rose to go.

“You’re always very prompt about leaving me.”

“I﻿—I try to be,” he said. “It isn’t easy to be careful not to risk everything by giving myself a little more at a time. If I ever saw you look tired﻿—”

===Chinese===
“可他没死，”比布斯说。这时隔壁一只嘶哑的小钟噼啪响起一串声音，数一数，是十下。“每回我同你在一处，他就钻进那口钟里去。”他深深叹了口气，起身告辞。

“你回回离开我，都走得那么干脆。”

“我——我是尽量这样做，”他说。“要处处小心，不让自己一回多讨一小点、免得把一切都拿去冒险，可不容易。哪一回要是我看见你露出倦容——”

===Original===
“Have you ever?”

“Not yet. You always look﻿—you always look﻿—”

“How?”

===Chinese===
“你见过吗？”

“还没有。你看上去总是——你看上去总是——”

“总是什么？”

===Original===
“Carefree. That’s it. Except when you feel sorry for me about something, you always have that splendid look. It puts courage into people to see it. If I had a struggle to face I’d keep remembering that look﻿—and I’d never give up! It’s a brave look, too, as though gaiety might be a kind of gallantry on your part, and yet I don’t quite understand why it should be, either.” He smiled quizzically, looking down upon her. “Mary, you haven’t a secret sorrow, have you?”

For answer she only laughed.

“No,” he said; “I can’t imagine you with a care in the world. I think that’s why you were so kind to me﻿—you have nothing but happiness in your own life, and so you could spare time to make my troubles turn to happiness, too. But there’s one little time in the twenty-four hours when I’m not happy. It’s now, when I have to say good night. I feel dismal every time it comes﻿—and then, when I’ve left the house, there’s a bad little blankness, a black void, as though I were temporarily dead; and it lasts until I get it established in my mind that I’m really beginning another day that’s to end with *you* again. Then I cheer up. But now’s the bad time﻿—and I must go through it, and so﻿—good night.” And he added with a pungent vehemence of which he was little aware, “I hate it!”

===Chinese===
“无忧无虑。就是这个。除了你有时为我难过之外，你脸上总带着那种光彩照人的神气。人一见它，心里就生出勇气。我若有一场拼搏要面对，就会一直记着那副神气——那我永远不会认输！那又是一副勇敢的神气，仿佛那份快活在你算一种豪侠之举；可它为什么会是这样，我也一样不大明白。”他带着探询的笑意，低头望着她。“玛丽，你没有藏着什么暗中的伤心事吧？”

作为回答，她只是笑。

“没有，”他说；“我无法想象你会有什么烦恼。我想这正是你待我这么好的缘故——你自己生活里只有幸福，所以匀得出工夫来，把我的烦恼也变成幸福。可是一天二十四小时里头，有那么一小段，我是不快活的。就是眼下，我不得不说晚安的时候。每到这一刻，我就觉得凄惶——随后，出了你们家的门，心里便有一小片糟糕的空白，一团黑的虚空，仿佛我暂时死了一样；要一直等到我把一件事在心里坐实：我确实是在开始又一个白天，而这个白天还照样要以*你*来收尾——这才缓过来。然后我就打起了精神。可是眼下正是那段坏时辰——我总得熬过去，所以——晚安。”末了他又添了一句，那股尖利的激切连他自己也没大觉察：“我恨这一刻！”

===Original===
“Do you?” she said, rising to go to the door with him. But he stood motionless, gazing at her wonderingly.

“Mary! Your eyes are so﻿—” He stopped.

“Yes?” But she looked quickly away.

===Chinese===
“是吗？”她说着，起身要陪他到门口去。可他站着一动不动，满心诧异地望着她。

“玛丽！你的眼睛那么——”他住了口。

“怎么？”可她急忙把目光移开了。

===Original===
“I don’t know,” he said. “I thought just then﻿—”

“What did you think?”

“I don’t know﻿—it seemed to me that there was something I ought to understand﻿—and didn’t.”

===Chinese===
“我说不上来，”他说。“方才我忽然觉得——”

“觉得什么？”

“说不上来——我仿佛觉得有一样东西我本该懂得，却没有懂。”

===Original===
She laughed and met his wondering gaze again frankly. “My eyes are pleased,” she said. “I’m glad that you miss me a little after you go.”

“But tomorrow’s coming faster than other days if you’ll let it,” he said.

She inclined her head. “Yes. I’ll﻿—‘let it’!”

“Going to church,” said Bibbs. “It *is* going to church when I go with you!”

===Chinese===
她笑了，重新坦坦然然迎住他诧异的目光。“我的眼睛是高兴，”她说。“你走了以后会想我一小会儿，我很高兴。”

“可是只要你肯由着它，明天就会比别的日子来得更快，”他说。

她微微颔首。“好。我——‘由着它’！”

“做礼拜去，”比布斯说。“同你一道去，那*才*是做礼拜去！”

===Original===
She went to the front door with him; she always went that far. They had formed a little code of leave-taking, by habit, neither of them ever speaking of it; but it was always the same. She always stood in the doorway until he reached the sidewalk, and there he always turned and looked back, and she waved her hand to him. Then he went on, halfway to the New House, and looked back again, and Mary was not in the doorway, but the door was open and the light shone. It was as if she meant to tell him that she would never shut him out; he could always see that friendly light of the open doorway﻿—as if it were open for him to come back, if he would. He could see it until a wing of the New House came between, when he went up the path. The open doorway seemed to him the beautiful symbol of her friendship﻿—of her thought of him; a symbol of herself and of her ineffable kindness.

And she kept the door open﻿—even tonight, though the sleet and fine snow swept in upon her bare throat and arms, and her brown hair was strewn with tiny white stars. His heart leaped as he turned and saw that she was there, waving her hand to him, as if she did not know that the storm touched her. When he had gone on, Mary did as she always did﻿—she went into an unlit room across the hall from that in which they had spent the evening, and, looking from the window, watched him until he was out of sight. The storm made that difficult tonight, but she caught a glimpse of him under the streetlamp that stood between the two houses, and saw that he turned to look back again. Then, and not before, she looked at the upper windows of Roscoe’s house across the street. They were dark. Mary waited, but after a little while she closed the front door and returned to her window. A moment later two of the upper windows of Roscoe’s house flashed into light and a hand lowered the shade of one of them. Mary felt the cold then﻿—it was the third night she had seen those windows lighted and the shade lowered, just after Bibbs had gone.

But Bibbs had no glance to spare for Roscoe’s windows. He stopped for his last look back at the open door, and, with a thin mantle of white already upon his shoulders, made his way, gasping in the wind, to the lee of the sheltering wing of the New House.

===Chinese===
她陪他走到前门；她回回总送到这里。道别的一套小小规矩，是习惯悄悄替他们定下的，两个人谁也不曾提过一个字；可它回回都是一样。她总站在门道里，直到他走上人行道；他到了那里总要回身望一望，她便向他扬一扬手。随后他再往前走，走到去新宅的半路，又回头一望：玛丽已经不在门道里了，可门还开着，灯光泻了出来。仿佛她有意告诉他：她永远不会把他关在门外；他总能望见那扇敞开的门道里友好的灯光——仿佛那门是为他开着的，他若愿意，随时可以回来。他一直望得见它，直到走上小径、新宅的一翼横进视线中间。那扇敞开的门道，在他看来是她友谊的美丽象征——是她心中念着他的象征；是她本人、是她那难以言喻的善良的象征。

而她一直让门开着——今晚也一样，尽管冻雨夹着细雪直扑到她裸着的颈子和手臂上，她的褐色头发里落满了细小的白色星点。他回身望见她果然还在那里向他扬手，仿佛不知道风暴正刮在她身上，他的心猛地一跳。等他走远了，玛丽便照素来的做法——走进一间没点灯的屋子，与他们今晚聚坐的那间正好隔着门厅——凭窗望着他，直到望不见为止。今晚的风暴叫这件事费了难，可她还是瞥见了他的影儿，就在两宅之间那盏路灯底下，还看见他又回身望了一眼。直到这时——而不是在这以前——她才望向街对面罗斯科家的楼窗。窗子是黑的。玛丽等着，过了一小会儿，她关上前门，回到自己窗前。片刻之后，罗斯科家楼上的两扇窗忽然亮起灯来，一只手放下了一扇窗的窗帘。到这时，玛丽才觉出冷来——比布斯前脚刚走，那两扇窗便亮起灯、放下帘，今夜已是第三回了。

可比布斯没有余光分给罗斯科家的窗子。他停了停，最后回望一眼那扇敞开的门，肩头已经披了薄薄一层白，在风里喘着气，一路挨到新宅那一翼挡风的背风处。

===Original===
A stricken George, muttering hoarsely, admitted him, and Bibbs became aware of a paroxysm within the house. Terrible sounds came from the library: Sheridan cursing as never before; his wife sobbing, her voice rising to an agonized squeal of protest upon each of a series of muffled detonations﻿—the outrageous thumping of a bandaged hand upon wood; then Gurney, sharply imperious, “Keep your hand in that sling! Keep your hand in that sling, I say!”

“*Look!*” George gasped, delighted to play herald for so important a tragedy; and he renewed upon his face the ghastly expression with which he had first beheld the ruins his calamitous gesture laid before the eyes of Bibbs. “Look at ’at lamidal statue!”

Gazing down the hall, Bibbs saw heroic wreckage, seemingly Byzantine﻿—painted colossal fragments of the shattered torso, appallingly human; and gilded and silvered heaps of magnificence strewn among ruinous palms like the spoil of a barbarians’ battle. There had been a massacre in the oasis﻿—the Moor had been hurled headlong from his pedestal.

===Chinese===
给他开门的是乔治，一脸遭了大难的神气，哑着嗓子咕哝个不停；比布斯一进门便觉出，这座宅子里正发作着一场狂乱。图书室里传出可怕的声音：谢里丹在咒骂，骂得比哪一回都凶；他的老婆在啜泣，而那一连串闷闷的爆响——那只缠了绷带的手对木头捶下去的放肆闷响——每一记都逼得她的嗓音拔成一声痛苦的、抗议的尖叫；随后是格尼，又急又厉：“手放回吊带里去！我说了，手放回吊带里去！”

“*瞧！*”乔治喘着气说——能为这样一桩了不得的惨祸充当报信人，他乐得很；脸上重新摆出那副惨白的神情，正是他头一眼望见那片废墟时的神情——那片废墟，正是他那只闯了祸的手摆到比布斯眼前来的。“瞧那座拉米达尔像！”

比布斯顺着门厅望进去，望见一派英雄气概的残骸，看上去俨然拜占庭的遗物——躯干碎裂的巨大彩绘残片，人形逼真得骇人；一堆堆鎏金镀银的华彩物件，散乱在倾倒的棕榈之间，就像蛮族一场恶战掳来的战利品。绿洲里遭过一场屠杀——摩尔人像已经从座台上被一头掀翻在地。

===Original===
“He hit ’at ole lamidal statue,” said George. “*Pow!*”

“My father?”

“*Yes*suh! *Pow!* he hit ’er! An’ you’ ma run tell me git doctuh quick ’s I kin telefoam﻿—she sho’ you’ pa goin’ bus’ a blood-vessel. He ain’t takin’ on ’tall *now*. He ain’t nothin’ ’tall to what he was ’while ago. You done miss’ it, Mist’ Bibbs. Doctuh got him all quiet’ down, to what he was. *Pow!* he hit’er! Yessuh!” He took Bibbs’s coat and proffered a crumpled telegraph form. “Here what come,” he said. “I pick ’er up when he done stompin’ on ’er. You read ’er, Mist’ Bibbs﻿—you’ ma tell me tuhn ’er ovuh to you soon’s you come in.”

===Chinese===
“他揍了那座老拉米达尔像，”乔治说。“*砰！*”

“我父亲？”

“*是啦*，少爷！*砰*！他揍了它！您妈妈跑来叫俺快打电话去请大夫——她说您爸爸非爆了血管不可。他*这会儿*倒不闹了。比起刚才那一阵，他如今这算不了一回事儿。您没赶上瞧，比布斯少爷。大夫把他安抚得平平的，比先前好多了。*砰*！他揍了它！是啦，少爷！”他接过比布斯的外套，递上一张揉皱的电报单。“来的就是这个，”他说。“他把它踩够了，俺才捡起来的。您念吧，比布斯少爷——您妈妈交代了，您一进门就把她交给您。”

===Original===
Bibbs read the telegram quickly. It was from New York and addressed to Mrs. Sheridan.

> Sure you will all approve step have taken as was so wretched my health would probably suffered severely Robert and I were married this afternoon thought best have quiet wedding absolutely sure you will understand wisdom of step when you know Robert better am happiest woman in world are leaving for Florida will wire address when settled will remain till spring love to all father will like him too when knows him like I do he is just ideal.
> Edith Lamhorn.

===Chinese===
比布斯把电报很快读完了。电报从纽约打来，收报人是谢里丹太太。

> 深信你们全家必都赞许我所采取的步骤因我实在苦恼不堪健康势必大受损害罗伯特与我已于今日午后成婚认为最好办一个安静的婚礼等你们更了解罗伯特之后必定明白此举的明智我是世上最幸福的女人我们即动身去佛罗里达安顿后再电告地址将住到春天向全家问好父亲像我这样了解他之后也会喜欢他的他正是理想中人
> 伊迪丝·拉姆霍恩

## XXVI / 二十六

===Original===
George departed, and Bibbs was left gazing upon chaos and listening to thunder. He could not reach the stairway without passing the open doors of the library, and he was convinced that the mere glimpse of him, just then, would prove nothing less than insufferable for his father. For that reason he was about to make his escape into the gold-and-brocade room, intending to keep out of sight, when he heard Sheridan vociferously demanding his presence.

“Tell him to come in here! He’s out there. I heard George just let him in. Now you’ll *see*!” And tear-stained Mrs. Sheridan, looking out into the hall, beckoned to her son.

Bibbs went as far as the doorway. Gurney sat winding a strip of white cotton, his black bag open upon a chair near by; and Sheridan was striding up and down, his hand so heavily wrapped in fresh bandages that he seemed to be wearing a small boxing-glove. His eyes were bloodshot; his forehead was heavily bedewed; one side of his collar had broken loose, and there were bloodstains upon his right cuff.

===Chinese===
乔治去了，剩下比布斯对着满目的混沌，耳听着隆隆的雷声。他要走到楼梯口去，就非得经过书房那两扇敞开的门不可；而他深信，这会儿哪怕叫他父亲瞥上一眼，也断断是要受不了的。因此他正要溜进金漆锦缎的屋子里去避一避，打算不叫人瞧见，忽听得谢里丹大声大气地点名要他进去。

“叫他到这儿来！他就在外头呢。我听见乔治刚把他放进来。这会儿你就*瞧着*吧！”满脸泪痕的谢里丹太太朝门厅里望出去，向她的儿子招手。

比布斯走到门口就站定了。格尼坐着，正缠绕着一条白棉布绷带，黑药包在近旁一把椅子上敞着口；谢里丹则在屋里大步踱来踱去，那只手用新绷带裹得厚厚的，看上去就像戴着一只小号的拳击手套。他两眼布满血丝，额头上蒙着一层密密的汗珠，领子有一边散了开来，右袖口上沾着血渍。

===Original===
“*There’s* our little sunshine!” he cried, as Bibbs appeared. “*There’s* the hope o’ the family﻿—my lifelong pride and joy! I want﻿—”

“Keep you hand in that sling,” said Gurney, sharply.

Sheridan turned upon him, uttering a sound like a howl. “For God’s sake, sing another tune!” he cried. “You said you ‘came as a doctor but stay as a friend,’ and in that capacity you undertake to sit up and criticize *me*﻿—”

“Oh, talk sense,” said the doctor, and yawned intentionally. “What do you want Bibbs to say?”

“You were sittin’ up there tellin’ me I got ‘hysterical’﻿—‘hysterical,’ oh Lord! You sat up there and told me I got ‘hysterical’ over nothin’! You sat up there tellin’ me I didn’t have as heavy burdens as many another man you knew. I just want you to hear *this*. Now listen!” He swung toward the quiet figure waiting in the doorway. “Bibbs, will you come downtown with me Monday morning and let me start you with two vice-presidencies, a directorship, stock, and salaries? I ask you.”

===Chinese===
“*瞧*，咱们家的小太阳来了！”比布斯一露面他就嚷道。“*瞧*，这一家的指望——我一辈子的骄傲和欢喜！我要——”

“把你手放回吊带里去，”格尼厉声说。

谢里丹冲他转过身来，发出一声狼嗥似的叫喊。“看在老天爷份上，换个调门唱唱吧！”他吼道。“你说过你是‘以医生的身份来，以朋友的身份留下’的，好嘛，你就以那个身份自居，坐在这儿指摘起*我*来了——”

“唉，说点儿正经的吧，”医生说着，故意打了个哈欠。“你要比布斯说什么呢？”

“你坐在楼上对我讲，说我‘歇斯底里’了——歇斯底里，老天爷！你坐在楼上对我讲，说我是为着一点儿小事就‘歇斯底里’了！你坐在楼上对我讲，说我挑的担子，没有你认得的好些别的男人挑的重。我就是要你听听*这个*。现在你听着！”他猛地转向在门口等候的那个安静的身影。“比布斯，礼拜一早上，你肯不肯跟我下市区去，让我给你起个头：两个副总裁的位子、一个董事的席位、股票，还有薪水？我问你。”

===Original===
“No, father,” said Bibbs, gently.

Sheridan looked at Gurney and then faced his son once more.

“Bibbs, you want to stay in the shop, do you, at nine dollars a week, instead of takin’ up my offer?”

“Yes, sir.”

“And I’d like the doctor to hear: What’ll you do if I decide you’re too high-priced a workin’-man either to live in my house or work in my shop?”

“Find other work,” said Bibbs.

===Chinese===
“不，父亲，”比布斯温声说。

谢里丹看了格尼一眼，然后又一次把脸转向他的儿子。

“比布斯，你是宁可留在车间里，一礼拜挣九块钱，也不接我提的那个提议，是不是？”

“是的，先生。”

“我还要请大夫听听：要是我认定你这个做工的身价太贵，我的房子住不起你，我的厂子也雇不起你，你怎么办？”

“另找活儿干，”比布斯说。

===Original===
“There! You hear him for yourself!” Sheridan cried. “You hear what﻿—”

“Keep you hand in that sling! Yes, I hear him.”

Sheridan leaned over Gurney and shouted, in a voice that cracked and broke, piping into falsetto: “He thinks of bein’ a *plumber*! He wants to be a *plumber*! He told me he couldn’t *think* if he went into business﻿—he wants to be a plumber so he can *think*!”

He fell back a step, wiping his forhead with the back of his left hand. “There! That’s my son! That’s the only son I got now! That’s my chance to live,” he cried, with a bitterness that seemed to leave ashes in his throat. “That’s my one chance to live﻿—that thing you see in the doorway yonder!”

===Chinese===
“听见了吧！你亲耳听见他说的了！”谢里丹嚷道。“你听听他这——”

“把你手放回吊带里去！听见了，我听见了。”

谢里丹朝格尼探过身去，用一副劈裂走音、直窜假声的嗓子吼道：“他打算去当个*管子匠*！他要当*管子匠*！他对我说过，一进买卖行当他就*思想*不成——他要去当管子匠，好叫他能*思想*！”

他倒退一步，用左手背抹了抹额头。“瞧吧！这就是我的儿子！这就是我如今仅剩的一个儿子！这就是我活下去的指望，”他嚷道，那一腔苦涩仿佛在他喉咙里积下了灰烬。“那就是我唯一的活路——就是你在那边门口瞧见的那个东西！”

===Original===
Dr. Gurney thoughtfully regarded the bandage strip he had been winding, and tossed it into the open bag. “What’s the matter with giving Bibbs a chance to live?” he said, coolly. “I would if I were you. You’ve had *two* that went into business.”

Sheridan’s mouth moved grotesquely before he could speak. “Joe Gurney,” he said, when he could command himself so far, “are you accusin’ me of the responsibility for the death of my son James?”

“I accuse you of nothing,” said the doctor. “But just once I’d like to have it out with you on the question of Bibbs﻿—and while he’s here, too.” He got up, walked to the fire, and stood warming his hands behind his back and smiling. “Look here, old fellow, let’s be reasonable,” he said. “You were bound Bibbs should go to the shop again, and I gave you and him, both, to understand pretty plainly that if he went it was at the risk of his life. Well, what did he do? He said he wanted to go. And he did go, and he’s made good there. Now, see: Isn’t that enough? Can’t you let him off now? He wants to write, and how do you know that he couldn’t do it if you gave him a chance? How do you know he hasn’t some message﻿—something to say that might make the world just a little bit happier or wiser? He *might*﻿—in time﻿—it’s a possibility not to be denied. Now he can’t deliver any message if he goes down there with you, and he won’t *have* any to deliver. I don’t say going down with you is likely to injure his health, as I thought the shop would, and as the shop did, the first time. I’m not speaking as doctor now, anyhow. But I tell you one thing I know: if you take him down there you’ll kill something that I feel is in him, and it’s finer, I think, than his physical body, and you’ll kill it deader than a doornail! And so why not let it live? You’ve about come to the end of your string, old fellow. Why not stop this perpetual devilish fighting and give Bibbs his chance?”

===Chinese===
格尼医生若有所思地端详了一阵手里那条缠了一半的绷带，随手把它丢进敞着口的药包。“给比布斯一个活下去的机会，又有什么不行呢？”他冷冷地说。“换了我，我就给。进买卖行当的，你已经有*两个*了。”

谢里丹的嘴古怪地扭动了几下，才说得出话来。“乔·格尼，”他到勉强能自持的地步才说，“你是想指控我，说我该对我儿子詹姆斯的死负责么？”

“我什么也不指控你，”医生说。“只是比布斯这个问题，我想跟你当面掰扯一回清楚——而且还趁他本人就在这儿。”他站起身，走到炉火跟前，背着手站着烤火，脸上带笑。“我说，老伙计，咱们讲讲道理吧，”他说。“是你铁定了心要比布斯再进车间，我当时就跟你和他两个人都交待得明明白白：他要是去，便是拿性命去冒风险。那么，他做了什么呢？他说他愿意去。他就去了，而且在那儿也干出了名堂。现在你瞧：这还不够么？如今你还不能放他一马么？他想写作，你怎么知道给了他机会他就写不成？你怎么知道他就没有一点要传达的话——没有一些要说出来的东西，兴许能让这个世界快活那么一点儿、明白那么一点儿？他*兴许有*——假以时日——这个可能是驳不倒的。可如今他要是跟你下了那边去，他就传不出什么话了，而且他也不会*有*什么话可传了。我不是说，跟你下那边去就会像当初我以为车间会的那样伤他的身子——头一回，那车间确实伤了。反正我现在也不是以医生的身份说话。可有一件事我知道，我告诉你：你把他带到那边去，你就会杀死一样东西，我觉出那东西在他身上，而且依我看，它比他的肉身还要精美；你会把它杀死，死得比门钉还死透！那么，干吗不让它活着呢？老伙计，你这根绳子也快拉到头儿了。干嘛不停下这场没完没了的、见鬼的争斗，给比布斯一个机会呢？”

===Original===
Sheridan stood looking at him fixedly. “What ‘fighting’?”

“Yours﻿—with nature.” Gurney sustained the daunting gaze of his fierce antagonist equably. “You don’t seem to understand that you’ve been struggling against actual law.”

“What law?”

“Natural law,” said Gurney. “What do you think beat you with Edith? Did Edith, herself, beat you? Didn’t she obey without question something powerful that was against you? *Edith* wasn’t against you, and you weren’t against *her*, but you set yourself against the power that had her in its grip, and it shot out a spurt of flame﻿—and won in a walk! What’s taken Roscoe from you? Timbers bear just so much strain, old man; but *you* wanted to send the load across the broken bridge, and you thought you could bully or coax the cracked thing into standing. Well, you couldn’t! Now here’s Bibbs. There are thousands of men fit for the life you want him to lead﻿—and so is he. It wouldn’t take half of Bibbs’s brains to be twice as good a business man as Jim and Roscoe put together.”

===Chinese===
谢里丹站在那儿，直勾勾地望着他。“什么‘争斗’？”

“你自己的——跟大自然的。”格尼平平静静地承受住了他那凶猛对手逼人的目光。“你似乎不明白，你一直在跟实实在在的法则角力。”

“什么法则？”

“自然法则，”格尼说。“你当伊迪丝那桩事，是叫什么打败的？是伊迪丝自己打败了你么？她不是毫不犹豫地服从了一种跟你作对的强大力量么？*伊迪丝*没有跟你作对，你也没有跟她作对，可你偏要跟那股把她攥在掌心里的力量作对，它便喷出一股火苗——不费吹灰之力就赢了！把罗斯科从你身边夺走的又是什么？木料的承力是有个定数的，老哥；可*你*偏要把载重赶过那座断了的桥，还当靠吓唬或是哄骗，就能叫那根裂了缝的东西撑住立稳。哼，你办不到！如今又轮到比布斯。配得上你要他过的那种日子的男人有千千万万——他也配得上。使出比布斯不到一半的脑子，就能做一个比吉姆和罗斯科加在一块儿还好上一倍的好买卖人。”

===Original===
“*What!*” Sheridan goggled at him like a zany.

“Your son Bibbs,” said the doctor, composedly, “Bibbs Sheridan has the kind and quantity of gray matter that will make him a success in anything﻿—if he ever wakes up! Personally I should prefer him to remain asleep. I like him that way. But the thousands of men fit for the life you want him to lead aren’t fit to do much with the life he *ought* to lead. Blindly, he’s been fighting for the chance to lead it﻿—he’s obeying something that begs to stay alive within him; and, blindly, he knows you’ll crush it out. You’ve set your will to do it. Let me tell you something more. You don’t know what you’ve become since Jim’s going thwarted you﻿—and that’s what was uppermost, a bafflement stronger than your normal grief. You’re half mad with a consuming fury against the very self of the law﻿—for it was the very self of the law that took Jim from you. That was a law concerning the cohesion of molecules. The very self of the law took Roscoe from you and gave Edith the certainty of beating you; and the very self of the law makes Bibbs deny you tonight. The *law* beats you. Haven’t you been whipped enough? But you want to whip the law﻿—you’ve set yourself against it, to bend it to your own ends, to wield it and twist it﻿—”

The voice broke from Sheridan’s heaving chest in a shout. “Yes! And by God, I will!”

===Chinese===
“*什么！*”谢里丹直眉瞪眼地望着他，活像个呆子。

“你儿子比布斯，”医生不动声色地说，“比布斯·谢里丹长着那样的脑髓，质地和分量都足以叫他在随便什么事情上成功——只要他有朝一日醒过来！就我个人说，我倒情愿他一直睡着。我就喜欢他那个样子。可是那配得上你要他过的那种日子的千千万万的人，拿他*本该*过的那种日子却做不出多少名堂。他一直懵懵懂懂地为争得那种日子的机会而抗争——他是在听命于他体内一样哀求着要活下去的东西；而且他懵懵懂懂地也知道，你会把它扼灭的。你已经立下你的意志，非要这么办不可。让我再告诉你一件事。自打吉姆的死挫折了你，你变成了什么样子，你自己并不知道——而盘踞在最上头的正是那个：一种比你正常的哀恸更烈的挫败迷惘。你给一股焚心蚀骨的怒火气得半疯，那怒火烧的是法则的本体——因为把吉姆从你手里夺去的，正是法则的本体。那是一条关于分子凝聚的法则。法则的本体从你身边夺走了罗斯科，又把胜过你的把握给了伊迪丝；今晚叫比布斯违抗你的，也正是法则的本体。打败你的是*法则*。你挨的鞭子还不够么？可你倒要执鞭去抽法则——你把自己摆到了它的对面，要叫它屈就你的私意，要把它攥在手里使唤、把它拧弯——”

那声音从谢里丹起伏的胸膛里迸成一声吼叫。“不错！老天在上，我就要拧弯它！”

===Original===
“So Ajax defied the lightning,” said Gurney.

“I’ve heard that dam’-fool story, too,” Sheridan retorted, fiercely. “That’s for chuldern and niggers. It ain’t twentieth century, let me tell you! ‘Defied the lightning,’ did he, the jackass! If he’d been half a man he’d ’a’ got away with it. *We* don’t go showin’ off defyin’ the lightning﻿—we hitch it up and make it work for us like a black-steer! A man nowadays would just as soon think o’ defyin’ a woodshed!”

“Well, what about Bibbs?” said Gurney. “Will you be a really big man now and﻿—”

“Gurney, you know a lot about bigness!” Sheridan began to walk to and fro again, and the doctor returned gloomily to his chair. He had shot his bolt the moment he judged its chance to strike center was best, but the target seemed unaware of the marksman.

===Chinese===
“埃阿斯（Ajax）当年就是这样向闪电挑战的，”格尼说。

“那个该死的蠢故事我也听说过，”谢里丹凶巴巴地顶了回去。“那是讲给娃儿们跟黑鬼听的。我告诉你，那一套可不是二十世纪的玩意儿！‘向闪电挑战’，他还真干了哪，那头蠢驴！他要有半个男子汉的斤两，早就把这事干成了。*我们*可不去逞那门子能、跟闪电斗气——我们把闪电拴上套，叫它像头黑阉牛似的给我们干活！如今的男子汉要动这念头，还不如去动念头跟一间柴棚挑战呢！”

“那么，比布斯怎么办？”格尼说。“你现在肯不肯做一回真正的大人物，把——”

“格尼，你可真算得懂庞大啊！”谢里丹又开始踱来踱去，医生闷闷地回到自己的椅子上。他在自料那一箭最有指望命中靶心的当儿放出了箭，那靶子却似乎压根儿没觉察出有个射手。

===Original===
“I’m tryin’ to make a big man out o’ that poor truck yonder,” Sheridan went on, “and you step in, beggin’ me to let him be Lord knows what﻿—I don’t! I suppose you figure it out that now I got a *son-in-law*, I mightn’t need a son! Yes, I got a son-in-law now﻿—a spender!”

“Oh, put your hand back!” said Gurney, wearily.

There was a bronze inkstand upon the table. Sheridan put his right hand in the sling, but with his left he swept the inkstand from the table and halfway across the room﻿—a comet with a destroying black tail. Mrs. Sheridan shrieked and sprang toward it.

“Let it lay!” he shouted, fiercely. “Let it lay!” And, weeping, she obeyed. “Yes, sir,” he went on, in a voice the more ominous for the sudden hush he put upon it. “I got a spender for a son-in-law! It’s wonderful where property goes, sometimes. There was ole man Tracy﻿—you remember him, Doc﻿—J. R. Tracy, solid banker. He went into the bank as messenger, seventeen years old; he was president at forty-three, and he built that bank with his life for forty years more. He was down there from nine in the morning until four in the afternoon the day before he died﻿—over eighty! Gilt edge, that bank? It was diamond edge! He used to eat a bag o’ peanuts and an apple for lunch; but he wasn’t stingy﻿—he was just livin’ in his business. He didn’t care for pie or automobiles﻿—he had his bank. It was an institution, and it come pretty near bein’ the beatin’ heart o’ this town in its time. Well, that ole man used to pass one o’ these here turned-up-nose and turned-up-pants cigarette boys on the streets. Never spoke to him, Tracy didn’t. Speak to him? God! he wouldn’t ’a’ coughed on him! He wouldn’t ’a’ let him clean the cuspidors at the bank! Why, if he’d ’a’ just seen him standin’ in *front* the bank he’d ’a’ had him run off the street. And yet all Tracy was doin’ every day of his life was workin’ for that cigarette boy! Tracy thought it was for the bank; he thought he was givin’ his life and his lifeblood and the blood of his brain for the bank, but he wasn’t. It was every bit﻿—from the time he went in at seventeen till he died in harness at eighty-three﻿—it was every last lick of it just slavin’ for that turned-up-nose, turned-up-pants cigarette boy. *And Tracy didn’t even know his name!* He died, not ever havin’ heard it, though he chased him off the front steps of his house once. The day after Tracy died his old-maid daughter married the cigarette﻿—and there *ain’t* any Tracy bank any more! And now”﻿—his voice rose again﻿—“and now *I* got a cigarette son-in-law!”

===Chinese===
“我正要把那边那个可怜的破烂货造就成一个大人物呢，”谢里丹接着说，“你倒插进一脚来，求我随他去当个老天才知道的什么东西——我才不依！我猜，你心里打的好算盘是：如今我有了个*女婿*，兴许就用不着儿子了！不错，我如今是有个女婿了——一个撒钱大爷！”

“唉，把你的手放回去！”格尼有气无力地说。

桌子上摆着一座青铜墨水台。谢里丹把右手放进了吊带，左手却把那墨水台从桌上一扫，横着飞过半间屋子——一颗拖着毁灭黑尾的彗星。谢里丹太太尖叫一声，朝它扑过去。

“让它搁着！”他凶巴巴地吼道。“让它搁着！”她流着泪依从了。“可不是，先生，”他接着说下去，那嗓音被他骤然压得死寂，愈发显得不祥。“我摊上个撒钱大爷做女婿！钱财这东西的去处，有时候可真叫人开眼。从前有位特雷西老头儿——你记得他的，大夫——J·R·特雷西（J. R. Tracy），根基扎实的银行家。他十七岁进银行当听差，四十三岁当上行长，打那以后又拿四十年的性命经营起那家银行。他死的前一天，还从早上九点一直守到下午四点——八十多岁的人了！金边，那家银行？它是钻石边的！他素来的午饭是一袋花生加一个苹果；可他并不抠门——他不过是活在他的买卖里罢了。馅饼他不爱，汽车他也不爱——他有他的银行。那是一家大机构，在它的年月里，差不多就算这座城跳动的心脏。咳，那位老人家在街上，常打一种翘鼻子、翘裤脚的烟卷儿小子身边走过。从没跟他说过话，特雷西没有。跟他说话？老天！他连咳嗽都断不会朝着他咳嗽！他连银行的痰盂都不会让他来擦！嘿，只要叫他瞧见这小子站在银行*门前*，他就会叫人把他撵出街去。可是特雷西一辈子每一天在做的事，全都是给那个烟卷儿小子卖命！特雷西还道是为着银行呢；他还道他是在把他的性命、他的心血、他的脑血一滴一滴献给银行，其实不是。那一切——打他十七岁进银行，到八十三岁殉职在任上——那一切从头到尾、一分一毫，全是在给那个翘鼻子、翘裤脚的烟卷儿小子当牛做马。*而特雷西竟连他的名字都不知道！*他到死也没听人说起过那个名字，虽然他有一回把这小子从自家门前的台阶上撵跑过。特雷西死后第二天，他的老姑娘女儿就嫁给了那个烟卷儿——如今*再没有*什么特雷西银行了！而如今”——他的声音又高起来——“如今*我*摊上了一个烟卷儿女婿！”

===Original===
Gurney pointed to the flourishing right hand without speaking, and Sheridan once more returned it to the sling.

“My son-in-law likes Florida this winter,” Sheridan went on. “That’s good, and my son-in-law better enjoy it, because I don’t think he’ll be there next winter. They got twelve-thousand dollars to spend, and I hear it can be done in Florida by rich sons-in-law. When Roscoe’s woman got me to spend that much on a porch for their new house, Edith wouldn’t give me a minute’s rest till I turned over the same to her. And she’s got it, besides what I gave her to go East on. It’ll be gone long before this time next year, and when she comes home and leaves the cigarette behind﻿—for good﻿—she’ll get some more. *My* name ain’t Tracy, and there ain’t goin’ to be any Tracy business in the Sheridan family. And there ain’t goin’ to be any college foundin’ and endowin’ and trusteein’, nor God-knows-what to keep my property alive when I’m gone! Edith’ll be back, and she’ll get a girl’s share when she’s through with that cigarette, but﻿—”

“By the way,” interposed Gurney, “didn’t Mrs. Sheridan tell me that Bibbs warned you Edith would marry Lamhorn in New York?”

Sheridan went completely to pieces: he swore, while his wife screamed and stopped her ears. And as he swore he pounded the table with his wounded hand, and when the doctor, after storming at him ineffectively, sprang to catch and protect that hand, Sheridan wrenched it away, tearing the bandage. He hammered the table till it leaped.

===Chinese===
格尼一声不响，指了指那只正挥得欢势的右手，谢里丹便又一次把它放回吊带里去。

“我女婿今年冬天喜欢上佛罗里达了，”谢里丹接着说。“那很好，我女婿顶好趁兴玩个痛快，因为依我看，明年冬天他到不了那儿了。他们手头有一万二千块钱好花；我听说，有钱的女婿在佛罗里达是花得掉这个数的。当初罗斯科家那个女人哄得我在他们新房的门廊上花了那么一笔数，伊迪丝就不让我有一分钟的安生，直到我把同样的一笔也拨给了她才罢。钱她到手了，还不算我给她动身上东部去的那一笔。还不到明年这个时节，那笔钱就会花得精光；等她回家来，把那个烟卷儿甩在身后——永远甩脱——她还会再得着一些。*我*可不姓特雷西，谢里丹家里也决计不会有出什么特雷西买卖的一天。也决计不会有什么创建大学、捐资设学、挂名董事，或是老天才知道的什么名堂，好在我闭眼之后叫我的产业继续活着！伊迪丝会回来的；等她跟那个烟卷儿了结清楚了，她自会拿到做闺女的那一份，可是——”

“对了，”格尼插进来说，“谢里丹太太不是告诉过我，比布斯早就警告过你，伊迪丝会在纽约跟拉姆霍恩成婚么？”

谢里丹当场整个儿垮了：他破口咒骂，他老婆尖叫着捂住了两只耳朵。他一面咒骂，一面用那只伤了的手捶着桌子；医生冲他发作了半晌不见效，便纵身扑上去，要捉住那只手护住它；谢里丹猛地把手一挣，把绷带扯脱了。他把桌子捶得直跳。

===Original===
“Fool!” he panted, choking. “If he’s shown gumption enough to guess right the first time in his life, it’s enough for me to begin learnin’ him on!” And, struggling with the doctor, he leaned toward Bibbs, thrusting forward his convulsed face, which was deathly pale. “My name ain’t Tracy, I tell you!” he screamed, hoarsely. “You give in, you stubborn fool! I’ve had my way with you before, and I’ll have my way with you now!”

Bibbs’s face was as white as his father’s, but he kept remembering that splendid look of Mary’s which he had told her would give him courage in a struggle, so that he would never give up.

“No. You can’t have your way,” he said. And then, obeying a significant motion of Gurney’s head, he went out quickly, leaving them struggling.

===Chinese===
“蠢货！”他噎得喘不上气，一字一字地挤出来。“他这辈子头一回露胆识就猜对了事情，光凭这个，也就够我拿他开教的了！”他一面跟医生扭作一团，一面朝比布斯探过身来，把那张抽搐的、死一样煞白的脸直凑上前。“我告诉你，我不姓特雷西！”他哑着嗓子尖叫。“你服个软吧，你这个死犟的蠢货！从前你都叫我就过范，这回你也得就范！”

比布斯的脸跟他父亲的一样白，可他不断地想起玛丽的那一副光彩照人的眼色——他曾对她说，在一场争斗里，那眼色会给他勇气，叫他永不认输。

“不。这回你叫不着我就范，”他说。随后，他依着格尼一个意味深长的摆头示意，快步走了出去，留下他们还在扭作一团。

## XXVII / 二十七

===Original===
Mrs. Sheridan, in a wrapper, noiselessly opened the door of her husband’s room at daybreak the next morning, and peered within the darkened chamber. At the old house they had shared a room, but the architect had chosen to separate them at the New, and they had not known how to formulate an objection, although to both of them something seemed vaguely reprehensible in the new arrangement.

Sheridan did not stir, and she was withdrawing her head from the aperture when he spoke.

“Oh, I’m *awake*! Come in, if you want to, and shut the door.”

===Chinese===
第二天清晨，天刚蒙蒙亮，谢里丹太太穿着一件宽大的晨衣，悄没声儿地推开她丈夫卧房的门，朝那间遮暗了的屋子里张望。在老宅，两口子向来是同住一间的；到了新宅，建筑师却自作主张把他们分了开，两口子又不知道该怎么表示反对，虽说这新的安排在他们两人心里，都觉得有点儿说不清道不明地不大妥当。

谢里丹一动没动。她正要从门缝里缩回脑袋，他却开了口。

“噢，我*醒着*呢！你愿意就进来，把门带上。”

===Original===
She came and sat by the bed. “I woke up thinkin’ about it,” she explained. “And the more I thought about it the surer I got I must be right, and I knew you’d be tormentin’ yourself if you was awake, so﻿—well, you got plenty other troubles, but I’m just sure you ain’t goin’ to have the worry with Bibbs it looks like.”

“You *bet* I ain’t!” he grunted.

“Look how biddable he was about goin’ back to the Works,” she continued. “He’s a right good-hearted boy, really, and sometimes I honestly have to say he seems right smart, too. Now and then he’ll say something sounds right bright. ’Course, most always it doesn’t, and a good deal of the time, when he says things, why, I have to feel glad we haven’t got company, because they’d think he didn’t have any gumption at all. Yet, look at the way he did when Jim﻿—when Jim got hurt. He took right hold o’ things. ’Course he’d been sick himself so much and all﻿—and the rest of us never had, much, and we were kind o’ green about what to do in that kind o’ trouble﻿—still, he did take hold, and everything went off all right; you’ll have to say that much, papa. And Dr. Gurney says he’s got brains, and you can’t deny but what the doctor’s right considerable of a man. He acts sleepy, but that’s only because he’s got such a large practice﻿—he’s a pretty wide-awake kind of a man some ways. Well, what he says last night about Bibbs himself bein’ asleep, and how much he’d amount to if he ever woke up﻿—that’s what I got to thinkin’ about. You heard him, papa; he says, ‘Bibbs’ll be a bigger business man than what Jim and Roscoe was put together﻿—if he ever wakes up,’ he says. Wasn’t that exactly what he says?”

===Chinese===
她走过去，在床边坐下。“我一想这事，人就醒了，”她解释说。“越想越觉得自己准没错，又知道你要是醒着，心里准得受煎熬，所以——喏，你别的烦心事已经够多了，可我敢断定，比布斯这事瞧着要让你操心，其实呀，你操不着的。”

“*我才不会呢*！”他哼了一声。

“你瞧他多听话，让回厂里去，他就回厂里去，”她接着说。“他真是个心眼儿顶好的孩子，说实在的，有时候我凭良心说，也不得不认他瞧着还挺机灵。时不时地，他也会冒出一两句听着真亮堂的话。当然喽，多半时候不是那样，而且好多时候，他一开口，唉，我就得暗自庆幸咱们家没有客人，不然人家要当他一点儿出息也没有了。可是你瞧瞧吉姆——你瞧吉姆出事那会儿他那股劲儿。他马上就把事情抓在手里了。当然喽，他自己害过那么久的病，又这又那的——咱们这些人一向没怎么害过病，碰上那种祸事该怎么办，都有点儿抓瞎——可他还是把事情抓起来了，样样都顺顺当当地过去了；这话你总得认一认吧，爸爸。还有格尼医生说他有脑子，医生是位很了得的人物，这个你驳不倒。他瞧着成天价睡不醒，那不过是因为他的诊务太忙——论起来，他也是个挺有精神头的人。喏，他昨儿晚上说的那些，说比布斯自己还睡着，又说他一旦醒过来能有多大出息——我琢磨的就是这个话。你是听见了的，爸爸；他说，‘比布斯会是个比吉姆和罗斯科两个加在一块儿还要强的生意人——只要他哪一天醒过来，’他是这么说的。他说的不是正是这个话？”

===Original===
“I suppose so,” said Sheridan, without exhibiting any interest. “Gurney’s crazier’n Bibbs, but if he wasn’t﻿—if what he says was true﻿—what of it?”

“Listen, papa. Just suppose Bibbs took it into his mind to get married. You know where he goes all the time﻿—”

“Oh, Lord, yes!” Sheridan turned over in the bed, his face to the wall, leaving visible of himself only the thick grizzle of his hair. “You better go back to sleep. He runs over there﻿—every minute she’ll let him, I suppose. Go back to bed. There’s nothin’ in it.”

===Chinese===
“我琢磨是，”谢里丹说，显不出一点儿兴趣。“格尼比比布斯还疯。可就算他不疯——就算他说的当真——那又怎么样？”

“你听着，爸爸。你就设想比布斯动了成家的念头。你知道他成天价往哪儿跑——”

“噢，天老爷，知道！”谢里丹在床上翻了个身，脸冲着墙，只把一头浓密的花白头发露在外面。“你顶好回去睡你的。他老上那边跑——我看只要人家姑娘肯让他去，他是一分钟也不歇的。回去睡觉吧。这里头什么名堂也没有。”

===Original===
“*Why* ain’t there?” she urged. “I know better﻿—there is, too! You wait and see. There’s just one thing in the world that’ll wake the sleepiest young man alive up﻿—yes, and make him *jump* up﻿—and I don’t care who he is or how sound asleep it looks like he is. That’s when he takes it into his head to pick out some girl and settle down and have a home and chuldern of his own. *Then*, I guess, he’ll go out after the money! You’ll see. I’ve known dozens o’ cases, and so’ve you﻿—moony, no-’count young men, all notions and talk, goin’ to be ministers, maybe or something; and there’s just this one thing takes it out of ’em and brings ’em right down to business. Well, I never could make out just what it is Bibbs wants to be, really; doesn’t seem he wants to be a minister exactly﻿—he’s so faraway you can’t tell, and he never *says*﻿—but I know this is goin’ to get him right down to common sense. Now, I don’t say that Bibbs has got the idea in his head yet﻿—’r else he wouldn’t be talkin’ that fool-talk about nine dollars a week bein’ good enough for him to live on. But it’s *comin’*, papa, and he’ll *jump* for whatever you want to hand him out. He will! And I can tell you this much, too: he’ll want all the salary and stock he can get hold of, and he’ll hustle to keep gettin’ more. That girl’s the kind that a young husband just goes crazy to give things to! She’s pretty and fine-lookin’, and things look nice on her, and I guess she’d like to have ’em about as well as the next. And I guess she isn’t gettin’ many these days, either, and she’ll be pretty ready for the change. I saw her with her sleeves rolled up at the kitchen window the other day, and Jackson told me yesterday their cook left two weeks ago, and they haven’t tried to hire another one. He says her and her mother been doin’ the housework a good while, and now they’re doin’ the cookin’, too. ’Course Bibbs wouldn’t know that unless she’s told him, and I reckon she wouldn’t; she’s kind o’ stiffish-lookin’, and Bibbs is too up in the clouds to notice anything like that for himself. They’ve never asked him to a meal in the house, but he wouldn’t notice that, either﻿—he’s kind of innocent. Now I was thinkin’﻿—you know, I don’t suppose we’ve hardly mentioned the girl’s name at table since Jim went, but it seems to me maybe if﻿—”

Sheridan flung out his arms, uttering a sound half-groan, half-yawn. “You’re barkin’ up the wrong tree! Go on back to bed, mamma!”

“Why am I?” she demanded, crossly. “Why am I barkin’ up the wrong tree?”

===Chinese===
“*怎么就*没有名堂？”她不依不饶。“我可比你明白——就是有名堂！你等着瞧吧。这世上只有一样东西，能把天底下睡得最死的年轻人弄醒——不错，还能叫他一骨碌*跳*起来——我才不管他是谁，也不管他瞧上去睡得有多么死。那就是他自个儿打定了主意，要挑中一个姑娘，安下家来，有自己的家、自己的娃儿们。*到了那会儿*，我猜，他就要出去追钱了！你瞧着吧。我知道的例子有几十个，你也知道——恍恍惚惚、不中用的年轻人，满脑子的怪念头，满嘴的空谈，不是要当牧师，就是要干点儿别的什么；偏偏就是这么一样东西能把他们点醒，把他们一下子拽到正经营生上。嗐，比布斯到底想成个什么人，我始终没琢磨透；瞧他那样子又不像是想当牧师——他那个人恍恍惚惚的，你猜不透，他自己也从来*不说*——可我知道，这一样东西准能把他拽回到常识上来。这会儿我倒不是说比布斯脑子里已经有这个念头了——要不然，他也不会胡说八道，讲一个礼拜九块钱够他过日子了。可是*快了*，爸爸，随你拿出什么来给他，他都会*跳*起来接。他会的！我还可以告诉你：凡是能到手的薪水跟股票，他全都要，还会拼死拼活地接着去挣。那样的姑娘，做年轻丈夫的就是发了疯似的要给她置办东西！她长得俊，齐整，什么穿戴到她身上都好看，我猜她跟随便哪一个人一样，也乐意有那么些好东西。我猜她这些日子也没得着多少，早就巴不得换个样儿了。前儿个我瞧见她站在厨房窗口那儿，袖子挽得高高的；昨天杰克逊又告诉我，他们家的厨娘两个礼拜前就走了，他们也压根儿没打算再雇。他说她跟她母亲料理家务已经好久了，如今连做饭也归了她们。当然喽，她要是不跟比布斯说，比布斯不会知道这些，我看她也不会说；她瞧着有几分拘板，比布斯又整天云里雾里，这样的事他自己留意不着。那家人从来没请他在家里吃过一顿饭，可这个他也不会留意——他就是有那么点儿天真。我这会儿正想着呢——你瞧，打吉姆去了以后，咱们在饭桌上怕是连那姑娘的名儿都没提过一回，可依我看，兴许要是——”

谢里丹把两条胳膊一甩，发出半声呻吟半声呵欠的声音。“你这是吠错了树啦！回去睡觉去，妈妈！”

“我怎么就吠错了？”她气恼地逼问。“我怎么就吠错了树？”

===Original===
“Because you are. There’s nothin’ in it.”

“I’ll bet you,” she said, rising﻿—“I’ll bet you he goes to church with her this morning. What you want to bet?”

“Go back to bed,” he commanded. “I *know* what I’m talkin’ about; there’s nothin’ in it, I tell you.”

She shook her head perplexedly. “You think because﻿—because Jim was runnin’ so much with her it wouldn’t look right?”

===Chinese===
“因为就是吠错了。这里头什么名堂也没有。”

“我跟你赌，”她说着站起身来——“我赌他今天早上就陪她上教堂去。你押什么？”

“回去睡觉，”他发话道。“我*知道*自己在说什么；我告诉你，这里头什么名堂也没有。”

她惶惑地摇摇头。“你是因为——因为吉姆跟她走得那么近，才觉得这个不合适，是不是？”

===Original===
“No. Nothin’ to do with it.”

“Then﻿—do you know something about it that you ain’t told me?”

“Yes, I do,” he grunted. “Now go on. Maybe I can get a little sleep. I ain’t had any yet!”

“Well﻿—” She went to the door, her expression downcast. “I thought maybe﻿—but﻿—” She coughed prefatorily. “Oh, papa, something else I wanted to tell you. I was talkin’ to Roscoe over the phone last night when the telegram came, so I forgot to tell you, but﻿—well, Sibyl wants to come over this afternoon. Roscoe says she has something she wants to say to us. It’ll be the first time she’s been out since she was able to sit up﻿—and I reckon she wants to tell us she’s sorry for what happened. They expect to get off by the end o’ the week, and I reckon she wants to feel she’s done what she could to kind o’ make up. Anyway, that’s what he said. I phoned him again about Edith, and he said it wouldn’t disturb Sibyl, because she’d been expectin’ it; she was sure all along it was goin’ to happen; and, besides, I guess she’s got all that foolishness pretty much out of her, bein’ so sick. But what I thought was, no use bein’ rough with her, papa﻿—I expect she’s suffered a good deal﻿—and I don’t think we’d ought to be, on Roscoe’s account. You’ll﻿—you’ll be kind o’ polite to her, won’t you, papa?”

===Chinese===
“不是。跟这个不相干。”

“那么——你是不是知道点儿什么，瞒着没跟我说？”

“是啊，我知道，”他哼了一声。“行了，去吧。兴许我还能眯上一会儿。我到这会儿还没合过眼呢！”

“那好——”她走到门口，一副垂头丧气的神情。“我还当兴许——可是——”她先干咳一声，清了清嗓子。“噢，爸爸，还有一件事得告诉你。昨儿晚上电报来的时候，我正跟罗斯科通着电话，就忘了跟你说，可是——喏，西比尔想今天下午过来。罗斯科说，她有话要跟咱们说。这是她能坐起身以来头一回出门——我猜她是想跟咱们说，出了那样的事，她心里过意不去。他们打算这个礼拜末就动身走，我猜她是想把自个儿能做的都做了，多少补一补，心里好受些。反正，他就是这么说的。伊迪丝的事我又给他打过一个电话，他说那搅扰不着西比尔，因为她早就料到了；她从头至尾就认定准会有这么一天；再说，我猜害了这一场大病，她那些糊涂念头也去得差不离了。可我想的是，犯不上对她厉害，爸爸——我看她也遭了不少罪——看在罗斯科的面子上，咱们也不该那样。你——你会对她客客气气的，好不好嘛，爸爸？”

===Original===
He mumbled something which was smothered under the coverlet he had pulled over his head.

“What?” she said, timidly. “I was just sayin’ I hoped you’d treat Sibyl all right when she comes, this afternoon. You will, won’t you, papa?”

He threw the coverlet off furiously. “I presume so!” he roared.

She departed guiltily.

===Chinese===
他把被头拉过头顶，咕咕哝哝说了句什么，话音全闷在被子里了。

“你说什么？”她怯怯地问。“我方才是说，西比尔今天下午来的时候，我希望你好好待人家。你会的吧，爸爸？”

他气冲冲地把被子一掀。“想必是吧！”他吼道。

她讪讪地退了出去。

===Original===
But if he had accepted her proffered wager that Bibbs would go to church with Mary Vertrees that morning, Mrs. Sheridan would have lost. Nevertheless, Bibbs and Mary did certainly set out from Mr. Vertrees’s house with the purpose of going to church. That was their intention, and they had no other. They meant to go to church.

But it happened that they were attentively preoccupied in a conversation as they came to the church; and though Mary was looking to the right and Bibbs was looking to the left, Bibbs’s leftward glance converged with Mary’s rightward glance, and neither was looking far beyond the other at this time. It also happened that, though they were a little jostled among groups of people in the vicinity of the church, they passed this somewhat prominent edifice without being aware of their proximity to it, and they had gone an incredible number of blocks beyond it before they discovered their error. However, feeling that they might be embarrassingly late if they returned, they decided that a walk would make them as good. It was a windless winter morning, with an inch of crisp snow over the ground. So they walked, and for the most part they were silent, but on their way home, after they had turned back at noon, they began to be talkative again.

“Mary,” said Bibbs, after a time, “am I a sleepwalker?”

===Chinese===
不过，倘若他当时接了她递上的赌注，赌比布斯那天早上会陪玛丽·弗特里斯上教堂，谢里丹太太就输定了。然而，比布斯和玛丽确确实实是从弗特里斯先生家动身的，为的是去上教堂。那是他们的打算，此外别无别的打算。他们是存心要去上教堂的。

可事情偏偏是这样：走到教堂跟前的时候，他们正专心致志地谈着话；玛丽朝右看，比布斯朝左看，比布斯朝左的目光恰恰同玛丽朝右的目光交汇到一处，这时候，谁的目光也没有越过对方看得更远。还有一桩凑巧：虽然他们在教堂左近的人群中间让人挤碰了几下，他们却从这座颇为惹眼的建筑旁边走了过去，全然不觉得自己已经到了它跟前；等发觉走岔了，两人已经鬼使神差地又走过了一条又一条叫人难以相信的街。不过，他们觉得这时候再折回去，只怕要迟得难为情，便拿定主意：散一回步也一样顶事。那是个没有风的冬日早晨，地上铺着一英寸厚的脆雪。于是他们走着，大半时候默默无言；可是晌午掉头往回走之后，两人又健谈起来。

“玛丽，”过了一会儿，比布斯说，“我是个梦游的人吗？”

===Original===
She laughed a little, then looked grave. “Does your father say you are?”

“Yes﻿—when he’s in a mood to flatter me. Other times, other names. He has quite a list.”

“You mustn’t mind,” she said, gently. “He’s been getting some pretty severe shocks. What you’ve told me makes me pretty sorry for him, Bibbs. I’ve always been sure he’s very big.”

“Yes. Big and﻿—blind. He’s like a Hercules without eyes and without any consciousness except that of his strength and of his purpose to grow stronger. Stronger for what? For nothing.”

===Chinese===
她轻轻笑了，随即神色郑重起来。“你父亲说你梦游吗？”

“是啊——趁他有兴致抬举我的时候。别的时候，就换别的名目。他那张单子可长着呢。”

“你别放在心上，”她温声说。“他这阵子受的打击不小。你跟我讲的那些，叫我怪替他难过的，比布斯。我一向深信，他这个人是非常庞大的。”

“是啊。庞大——而且是瞎的。他像赫拉克勒斯（Hercules），没有眼睛，除了自己的力气、除了那股要越长越强的意志之外，什么知觉都没有。更强，为了什么？什么也不为。”

===Original===
